## [Tree-Coupled A/B Testing Cuts Reward Queries in Multi-Policy Comparison](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12831) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces Tree-Coupled A/B Testing (TCAB), a feedback-sharing design that reduces reward queries in multi-policy comparison while preserving each policy's trajectory law. It achieves this by coupling policies on a tree structure and sharing rewards along matched edges. This method significantly reduces the cost of A/B/n testing for adaptive policies, which is crucial for online platforms comparing many policies under costly or risky interactions. It offers theoretical guarantees and practical improvements, potentially impacting experimentation in recommendation systems, pricing, and language-model agents. The number of reward queries satisfies the pathwise identity N(T)=T+ΣD_{e,t}, where D_{e,t} indicates a mismatch on tree edge e at round t, making the cost equal to T plus cumulative tree-edge total variation. For fixed J, sublinear pseudo-regret and almost-sure uniqueness of the oracle action imply E[N(T)]=T+o(T), versus JT for independent runs.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 14, 04:00

**Background**: A/B testing is a randomized experiment method used to compare two or more variants of a variable, often in user experience research. Contextual bandit policies are adaptive algorithms that select actions based on context to maximize reward, commonly used in online platforms. Traditional A/B/n testing runs each policy independently, leading to high reward query costs, which TCAB aims to reduce.

BrainWAM converts semantic reasoning and predictive world modeling into two specialized action-oriented pathways and aligns them at the level of compact action representations, avoiding the attention-allocation mismatch found in naive token-level combinations. However, simply combining them via joint attention can lead to an attention-allocation mismatch where semantic shortcuts dominate, suppressing predictive dynamics. BrainWAM draws inspiration from neuroscience, where complex behavior arises from coordination among specialized systems, to design a structured action-space coordination framework.

## [Compositional Curvature Theory for Probabilistic Circuits](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12869) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper proves that the Hessian trace in probabilistic circuits factorizes into circuit flow and local sharpness, and introduces an adaptive sharpness-aware regularizer that preserves closed-form EM updates. This work provides a theoretical explanation for why global sharpness regularization is depth-biased and can underfit, offering a targeted alternative that improves generalization in deep PCs. It advances the understanding of loss-surface geometry in tractable generative models. The decomposition shows each sum node's contribution to the Hessian trace equals its circuit flow times a local sharpness term. The proposed adaptive regularizer penalizes nodes based on intrinsic local curvature and empirically recovers generalization lost by global regularization.

**Background**: Probabilistic circuits (PCs) are generative models that support exact inference and, unlike deep neural networks, admit an exact and tractable measure of loss-surface curvature: the trace of the Hessian of the log-likelihood. Sharpness-aware minimization (SAM) is a deep learning technique that regularizes sharpness to improve generalization, but applying it globally to PCs can be misspecified due to their compositional structure.

## [SPARED: Adversarial RL Framework for Reasoning-Based AI Image Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12876) ⭐️ 8.0/10

SPARED introduces an adversarial reinforcement learning framework that pairs a diffusion-based image editor with a reasoning multimodal large language model (MLLM) to detect AI-generated images. The attacker creates challenging fakes to fool the detector, while the defender provides grounded verdicts, with both rewards designed to be shortcut-proof. This framework addresses critical shortcomings in current AI-generated image detectors, such as provenance shortcuts, templated rationales, and static decision boundaries. By forcing detectors to generalize and provide reasoning, SPARED could significantly improve media forensics and AI safety in an era of rapidly evolving generative models. The attacker is credited only when its edit is faithfully executed, and the defender only when its verdict is correct, ensuring both rewards are shortcut-proof. Although the explanation is never directly rewarded, its quality improves over rounds as a side effect of accuracy-only training, and the detector shows monotonic improvement across three external benchmarks.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 14, 04:00

**Background**: Adversarial reinforcement learning (ARL) is a deep RL approach that formulates control as a two-agent zero-sum Markov game, where an adversary steers the protagonist to unknown states. Multimodal large language models (MLLMs) like GPT-4V use LLMs as a brain to perform multimodal tasks, including reasoning about images. Shortcut learning occurs when models rely on spurious correlations in training data, such as pixel patterns, rather than the intended features, leading to poor generalization.

## [Latent World Model Planning Bottleneck Is the Objective, Not the Predictor](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12959) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new paper on arXiv (2608.12959) demonstrates that in latent world models, the planner's objective, not the predictor, limits long-horizon planning performance. By replacing only the objective in a reproduction of LeWorldModel on TwoRoom, they lifted goals reached at offset 100 from 26.0% to 98.0% without retraining or GPU use. This finding challenges a common assumption in model-based reinforcement learning that prediction accuracy is the primary bottleneck for planning. It suggests that improving the planner's objective could be a more effective and computationally cheaper path to better long-horizon planning, impacting the design of future world models and planning algorithms. The paper shows that cross-entropy-method planning minimizes squared latent distance, which tracks true distance at r=0.426, saturates by about eighty arena units, and decreases beyond a hundred and twenty, causing moving away from the goal to lower cost. A ridge probe recovers position from the frozen embedding at R^2 0.9922, indicating the information is present but not used effectively by the objective.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 14, 04:00

**Background**: Latent world models learn compressed representations of environments and use them for planning, often with the cross-entropy method (CEM) for optimization. The predictor is trained to forecast future latent states, and it's commonly assumed that prediction errors compound over long horizons, limiting planning. This paper investigates whether the bottleneck is actually the planner's objective function, which may not align with true task success.

This paper introduces HARD (Harness-based Autonomous Runtime Defense Evolution), a self-evolving runtime defense framework for LLM agents that automatically identifies intervention strategies and iteratively improves defense artifacts based on observed failure traces. It also develops a harness-level formulation of runtime defense that unifies existing interventions from a harness perspective. This work addresses a critical gap in LLM agent security by moving beyond handcrafted defenses to an autonomous evolution paradigm. It could significantly improve the security of deployed LLM agents while maintaining benign task utility, impacting AI/ML security and systems research. The framework is built on a harness-level formulation that characterizes how harness mechanisms enable defense construction. Extensive experiments show that HARD improves security performance over existing handcrafted defenses while preserving benign task utility, highlighting autonomous defense evolution as a promising new paradigm.

## [TEMPO: Makespan-Aware Load Balancing for MoE Serving](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13057) ⭐️ 8.0/10

TEMPO introduces a makespan-aware load balancing approach for expert-parallel MoE serving, modeling expert execution time with a max-affine profile that captures both memory-bound and compute-bound regimes. It formalizes the per-batch dispatch problem as an NP-hard fixed-charge makespan problem and provides a dispatcher that solves it in milliseconds, achieving up to 15.5% improvement in mixed regimes. This work addresses a critical performance bottleneck in MoE serving by showing that existing token-based or expert-based dispatchers are suboptimal due to ignoring the dual memory/compute regimes. The findings and the phase diagram can guide future system designs, potentially improving throughput and latency for large MoE models in production. The max-affine profile is t = max(a + bG, c + βN), where G is the number of activated replicas and N is the token count, with a regime transition around 156–168 tokens. TEMPO integrates with SGLang out-of-process, fusing dispatch with count collection into a single in-graph kernel, and on Testbed B, Qwen3-235B gains 4–6% throughput and ~15.6% p99 latency reduction, while DeepSeek-V3 shows only mechanism cost.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 14, 04:00

**Background**: In expert-parallel (EP) MoE serving, each layer synchronizes at the slowest GPU, so load balancing is crucial. Traditional dispatchers balance token counts or activated-expert counts, assuming expert execution time is linear in one of these, but real hardware shows a more complex behavior: below a certain token threshold, HBM weight streaming dominates, and above it, grouped GEMM introduces padding overhead. TEMPO's max-affine model captures both regimes, and the paper provides a phase diagram to predict when the approach yields gains.

The paper introduces LOB-ID, an embedding-based framework that adapts FID and MIND metrics to limit order book (LOB) data, using DeepLOB embeddings trained on four months of Level-2 data for five equities. It demonstrates that LOB-ID is stable and sensitive to distortions, and ranks five generative LOB models according to their structural fidelity. This work addresses a critical gap in evaluating generative LOB models, which often rely on stylized facts and statistics that miss joint temporal and cross-level structure. LOB-ID provides a more robust and sensitive metric, potentially improving model development and benchmarking in financial AI. The paper shows that MIND remains substantially more sensitive to both a moment-matching attack against FID and a deep-book perturbation that evades statistic-based evaluation. LOB-ID is stable across time, instruments, and embedding checkpoints, and rises monotonically under controlled distortions.

## [Audit Reveals Test-Time Budget Allocation Gains Are Often In-Sample Artifacts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13087) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new paper audits test-time budget allocation in neural combinatorial optimization (NCO) and finds that reported gains from non-uniform allocation are often artifacts of in-sample evaluation. Across three pretrained solvers on TSP-100, the oracle allocation showed a 2.2-2.6% gain in-sample but was indistinguishable from zero out-of-sample. This finding is significant because it exposes a methodological flaw in how NCO solvers are evaluated, potentially affecting the credibility of many published results. It provides a correction procedure and reporting checklist, which could help standardize evaluation practices and improve research integrity in the field. The study used three solvers (POMO, AM, SymNCO) on uniform TSP-100, and also tested under distribution shift with a pre-registered confirmatory experiment. Under shift, allocation guided by held-out statistics improved best-of-k by 11.5% (AM) and 12.0% (SymNCO), while a negative control (POMO) showed -0.3%, and an exploratory policy with a 20-sample probe retained 3.4% and 4.6% gains.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 14, 04:00

**Background**: Neural combinatorial optimization (NCO) uses neural networks to solve problems like the Traveling Salesman Problem (TSP), often by sampling multiple solutions per instance and selecting the best. Traditionally, the sample count is fixed per instance, but non-uniform allocation of a test-time budget could potentially improve efficiency. This paper audits such allocation methods, distinguishing between in-sample (evaluated on the same data used to compute the allocation) and out-of-sample (evaluated on new data) performance, which is crucial for assessing real-world generalization.

## [EgoMonth: Month-Level Egocentric Video Benchmark for Long-Term Memory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13113) ⭐️ 8.0/10

EgoMonth is introduced as the first month-level egocentric video understanding benchmark, comprising over 300 hours of first-person daily-life recordings from 20 participants spanning 20 to 120 days, paired with 1,443 human-crafted multiple-choice QA pairs. It features a cognitively grounded 14-task evaluation framework organized into three hierarchical cognitive levels: Schema Consolidation, Episodic Indexing, and Cascading Reasoning. This benchmark addresses a significant gap in evaluating long-term spatiotemporal memory in multimodal large language models (MLLMs), as existing benchmarks rely on web-sourced videos lacking inter-clip spatiotemporal continuity. The results reveal that even the best-performing model, Gemini 2.5 Pro, achieves only 71.8% macro-average accuracy, far below the human baseline of 94.2%, highlighting the need for architectures with genuine long-term memory. Several models perform near or below the 25% chance level on tasks such as Route Reasoning, Cross-view Spatial Reasoning, and Direction Judgement, indicating that current MLLMs function as lossy summarizers rather than faithful memorizers. The benchmark includes 20 participants and spans 20 to 120 days, with a 14-task framework designed to assess cognitive processes at different levels.

Existing long video benchmarks typically use web-sourced clips that lack the continuity of real-world experience, making it difficult to assess true spatiotemporal memory. ## [LipCache: Certified Semantic Caching for Edge Image Classification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13144) ⭐️ 8.0/10

LipCache introduces a certified semantic caching framework for edge image classification, using a Lipschitz-constrained GuardNet to compute per-sample certified reuse radii. This transforms cache hits from empirical threshold tests into geometric certification decisions, achieving up to 1.65x speedup on CIFAR, Tiny-ImageNet, and SVHN with minimal accuracy loss. This addresses a critical reliability issue in existing semantic caching methods, which rely on empirical similarity thresholds and can cause silent misclassifications near decision boundaries. By providing certified consistency, LipCache enables reliable cache-assisted inference at the edge, potentially improving latency and throughput for vision services without compromising accuracy. The framework introduces a lightweight GuardNet that maps inputs into a low-dimensional feature space under a Lipschitz constraint, computing reuse radii from the local classification margin and the spectral norm of the classification head. An enhanced GuardNet training recipe improves cache hit rates on Tiny-ImageNet while maintaining a 100% certified-consistency rate.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 14, 04:00

**Background**: Semantic caching is a technique that reuses results for similar queries based on semantic similarity, commonly used to reduce inference cost and latency in AI services. However, existing methods often use empirical thresholds, which can lead to incorrect reuse near decision boundaries. Lipschitz constraints bound how much a function's output can change with input perturbations, enabling certified robustness. Spectral normalization is a technique to enforce Lipschitz constraints by controlling the largest singular value of weight matrices.

## [TRAPSBench: VLMs Encode but Fail to Express Epistemic Restraint](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13167) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers introduced TRAPSBench, a procedurally generated video benchmark of 1,404 matched physics pairs, and the Penalized Epistemic Calibration Score (PECS) metric. Across 16 VLMs, spontaneous restraint was poor (best PECS 0.292), but linear probes decoded answerability from hidden states up to 0.91 AUROC, and steering a single-layer void direction causally induced or suppressed abstention. This work highlights a critical gap in VLM reliability: models internally know when to abstain but fail to express it, which is essential for AI safety in high-stakes applications. The findings suggest that output-stage interventions are needed to close this representation-output gap, potentially improving model calibration and trustworthiness. The benchmark includes answerable (control) videos, unanswerable (void) videos, and ill-posed (textual-void) questions. The failure is more pronounced in visual than textual uncertainty, with models detecting textual impossibility about 4x more readily than missing visual evidence. Results replicate across Qwen, Gemma, and LLaVA families.

**Background**: Epistemic restraint refers to a model's ability to abstain from answering when the input is ambiguous or insufficient. Vision-language models (VLMs) process both visual and textual inputs, and their calibration is crucial for reliable deployment. TRAPSBench uses procedurally generated physics scenarios to isolate the effect of a single targeted change that makes the outcome undeterminable, allowing systematic evaluation of abstention behavior.

GEM is a new generative embedding model that explicitly reasons about user intent and relevance criteria before encoding queries for retrieval, unifying generation and embedding in a single model. It outperforms its non-reasoning variant and matches baselines using substantially larger models on reasoning-intensive and instruction-following retrieval tasks. This addresses a critical gap between how users express complex information needs and how traditional retrievers rely on surface-level matching. By integrating reasoning into retrieval, GEM could improve search accuracy for complex queries and enable test-time compute scaling, potentially impacting information retrieval systems and AI applications. GEM first reasons over the query, then appends an embedding token to encode the enriched context for retrieval. Its generative nature allows test-time compute scaling via prompting to further enhance retrieval performance, and the code is available at an anonymous repository.

It periodically performs full-view computation to control error accumulation, and its sampler-consistent reconstruction preserves the denoising trajectory. ## [SciFigBench: Evaluating VLM Behavioral Reliability on Scientific Figures](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13267) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces SciFigBench, a diagnostic benchmark for evaluating vision-language models (VLMs) on scientific figure understanding, and proposes the A-R-I framework to assess behavioral reliability under uncertainty. The benchmark includes 250 figures with over 34,000 evaluation setups, and the study reveals that high accuracy does not guarantee reliable behavior, with GPT-5.2 hallucinating in 96% of unreadable cases while Gemini 3.1 Pro admits uncertainty in 71%. This work addresses a critical gap in VLM evaluation by focusing on behavioral reliability under uncertainty, which is essential for deploying models in scientific workflows where misleading or missing visual evidence can lead to serious errors. The findings challenge the assumption that high perception and reasoning accuracy suffice, prompting the community to consider new evaluation dimensions. SciFigBench contains 250 figures with high-quality human annotations, totaling over 600 hours of annotation effort, and is extended via image transformations, reasoning questions, resistance probes, caption-bias probes, and confirmed selective-blur targets to create over 34,000 evaluation setups. The A-R-I framework evaluates whether models acknowledge insufficient evidence (Admittance), resist misleading context (Resistance), and infer cautiously from partial information (Inductance), with results showing GPT-5.2 achieves MQM 91.6 and reasoning 78.4% but hallucinates in 96% of unreadable cases, while Gemini 3.1 Pro has MQM 90.2, reasoning 81.0%, admits uncertainty in 71% of cases, and achieves a resistance score of 0.91.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 14, 04:00

**Background**: Vision-language models (VLMs) are multimodal models that process both images and text, and they are increasingly used in scientific domains to interpret figures and diagrams. Existing benchmarks for VLMs primarily focus on perception and reasoning accuracy, measuring how well models describe and reason about visual content, but they often overlook how models behave when visual information is missing or misleading. Behavioral reliability under uncertainty is crucial for real-world applications, as models may hallucinate or make overconfident errors when faced with ambiguous or unreadable inputs. The A-R-I framework and SciFigBench aim to fill this gap by providing a structured evaluation of these behavioral aspects.

## [Mixture of Training: Modular Pretraining for Recomposable LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13277) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces Mixture of Training (MoT), a scaffolded modular pretraining procedure that partitions a Transformer into contiguous layer blocks, trains each block within a frozen pretrained aligner scaffold, and then recomposes them with an optional short adaptation pass. A proof-of-concept on a 1.3B Gemma-style model trained on C4 demonstrates that independently trained depth slices can be recomposed into a usable language model, achieving quality parity with monolithic pretraining. This work challenges the monolithic approach to LLM pretraining by suggesting that training can be decomposed into smaller, reusable units, potentially improving efficiency and scalability. If validated further, it could enable more flexible and resource-efficient training of large models, impacting both academic research and industrial applications. The MoT method uses a frozen pretrained aligner scaffold to train each block, and the recomposition includes an optional short end-to-end adaptation pass. The quality-parity schedule processes more aggregate tokens and has a shorter idealized layer-equivalent critical path after aligner preparation, but its effective compute advantage depends on reusing the aligner across runs.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 14, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models (LLMs) are typically pretrained as monolithic models, requiring massive compute and data. MoT proposes a modular alternative where training is split into independent blocks, each trained with the help of a frozen aligner scaffold, and then combined. This approach could reduce the critical path of training and allow for better reuse of trained components, but it is still at an early stage of research.

## [Physics-Informed Autoencoder Estimates DRT from EIS for Cell Monitoring](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13305) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A physics-informed convolutional autoencoder has been developed to estimate the distribution of relaxation times (DRT) directly from electrochemical impedance spectroscopy (EIS) data without per-spectrum tuning. It achieves range-normalized errors below 1.1% on real solid oxide fuel and electrolysis cell datasets. This method addresses the ill-posed inverse problem of DRT estimation, which is highly sensitive to regularization choices, and provides an interpretable latent space for condition monitoring. It could significantly improve diagnostics and state estimation in batteries, fuel cells, and electrolyzers, benefiting both electrochemistry and machine learning communities. The model embeds a discretized relation between impedance and DRT into the training process, ensuring impedance-consistent distributions. Decoder-probe analysis reveals that the latent representation is organized by relaxation timescale, and distances in latent space capture operating changes, hydrogen-shortage events, and long-term degradation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 14, 04:00

**Background**: Electrochemical impedance spectroscopy (EIS) measures a system's impedance over a range of frequencies, revealing energy storage and dissipation properties. The distribution of relaxation times (DRT) is a technique to deconvolve EIS data into a continuous distribution of time constants, aiding in identifying electrochemical processes. However, DRT estimation is an ill-posed inverse problem that traditionally requires careful regularization. Physics-informed neural networks integrate physical laws into the learning process, improving generalization and interpretability.

## [LLMs Show Gender-Associated Linguistic Bias in Responses](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13328) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new arXiv paper (2608.13328) demonstrates that LLMs systematically produce shorter, less sophisticated, and less formal responses when prompts contain linguistic features more common in women's speech, such as hedges, tag questions, and collective reference. This bias persists across three document types and four models, even after controlling for prompt complexity. This research reveals a novel and significant bias in LLMs that could lead to unequal treatment of users in AI-mediated professional communication, potentially reinforcing gender stereotypes. It highlights the challenge of mitigating such biases and calls for upstream consideration of linguistic variation in model development. The study found that explicit gender cues like sign-off names are encoded in the same representational space as linguistic dialect, but linguistic register has a much larger effect than names. Mechanistic analysis shows that these linguistic features are encoded in early transformer layers and entangled with other features, making post-hoc mitigation difficult.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 14, 04:00

**Background**: Linguistic features such as hedges, tag questions, and collective reference are often stereotypically associated with women's speech in sociolinguistics. LLMs are trained on vast amounts of text that may contain these stereotypes, leading to biased outputs. The paper's findings align with broader concerns about fairness and bias in AI systems, particularly in workplace communication where LLMs are increasingly used.

## [Deliberate Practice: Budget-Optimal Robot Skill Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13415) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces Deliberate Practice (DP), an active skill learning algorithm that computes a provably budget-optimal allocation of practice time to maximize expected cumulative reward in long-horizon robot tasks. It formulates the allocation problem as a bilinear program solvable with off-the-shelf solvers. This work addresses a critical challenge in autonomous robot learning: efficiently using limited practice time to acquire skills that improve long-horizon planning. It provides theoretical guarantees and practical validation, potentially influencing future research in active learning and robot skill acquisition. The algorithm estimates both the time needed to master skills and the cumulative reward of task plans unlocked by those skills. The bilinear program formulation allows exact computation of the optimal allocation, validated through simulated and real-world long-horizon manipulation experiments.

## [First Complete Characterization of Transformer Length Generalization on Regular Languages](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13433) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper establishes the first complete characterization of which regular languages transformers can length-generalize on, and provides a polynomial-time decision algorithm based on the size of the language's syntactic monoid. The results rely on an effective characterization of regular languages in C-RASP, a formalism expressing transformer length generalization. This work bridges formal language theory and deep learning, offering a theoretical foundation for understanding when transformers can generalize to longer sequences. It provides a practical decision tool that could guide model design and evaluation, impacting both theoretical research and practical applications in NLP. The paper generalizes classical Krohn-Rhodes decomposition theory from finite semigroups to the infinite additive group on the integers, enabling a characterization of C-RASP via iterated wreath products of the integers. Experiments across a broad test suite of regular languages confirm that the theory captures transformer length-generalization behavior more accurately than existing classifications.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 14, 04:00

**Background**: Transformers sometimes generalize to longer sequences than seen in training, but it was unclear which tasks admit such length generalization. Regular languages are a foundational class in formal language theory, and C-RASP is a recently introduced formalism that expresses which languages transformers length-generalize on. Classical algebraic tools like Krohn-Rhodes theory decompose finite semigroups into elementary components, but they are insufficient for C-RASP because its basic building block, unbounded counting, is not expressible by finite semigroups.

## [ContactGuard: Pre-Contact Failure Detection with Latent World Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13438) ⭐️ 8.0/10

ContactGuard introduces a pre-contact execution monitor for chunked visuomotor policies, predicting short-horizon consequences in latent visual space and aborting before contact if failure is likely. It uses an action-conditioned latent world model trained from unlabelled trajectories, avoiding pixel-level prediction. This addresses a critical gap in contact-rich manipulation, where failures are often detected only after contact, leading to damage or task failure. By predicting failures pre-contact, ContactGuard can improve safety and reliability of robotic systems without modifying the underlying policy, potentially benefiting a wide range of real-world applications. The latent world model predicts compact multi-view visual embeddings under planned actions, and a lightweight failure probe is trained from a small labelled set of pre-contact clips. At deployment, it anchors prediction before an imminent contact event and rolls the model forward under the policy's own actions, achieving higher accuracy than direct and corrupted-action ablations.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 14, 04:00

**Background**: Contact-rich manipulation tasks often involve wrist-mounted cameras that provide close views of the gripper-object interaction, but conventional detectors react only after contact, which may already cause pushing, missing, slipping, or disturbance. World models are predictive models that simulate future states given actions; latent world models operate in a compressed representation, making them more efficient than pixel-level prediction. Chunked visuomotor policies generate action chunks (sequences of actions) that are executed before replanning, providing a natural window for pre-contact monitoring.

## [UniTexture: Cross-Task Universal Adversarial Texture Attack on VLA Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13453) ⭐️ 8.0/10

UniTexture introduces a cross-task universal adversarial texture attack that uses a single textured 3D object to induce targeted action deviations in Vision-Language-Action (VLA) models across multiple tasks. It jointly optimizes the texture over a distribution of tasks, instructions, states, and viewpoints using a targeted action-space objective. This work reveals shared cross-task vulnerabilities in multitask VLA models, which can be systematically exploited through a single adversarial surface texture, posing safety risks for embodied AI applications. It highlights the need for robust defenses in VLA-based robotic systems. UniTexture reduces the mean task success rate from 90.0% under benign conditions to 48.4% under attack on OpenVLA and π0.5, and exhibits cross-suite and cross-model transfer without re-optimization. The attack backpropagates gradients from the policy's action outputs to surface texture parameters through a differentiable renderer.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 14, 04:00

**Background**: Vision-Language-Action (VLA) models are multimodal foundation models that integrate vision, language, and actions, enabling robots to follow natural language instructions. They are typically built by fine-tuning a vision-language model on robot trajectories. Adversarial texture attacks involve optimizing a texture on a 3D object to fool a model, and differentiable rendering allows gradients to flow from the model's output back to the texture parameters.

## [CAPRI: Contract-Aware Proof Repair for Isabelle](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13459) ⭐️ 8.0/10

CAPRI introduces a contract-aware proof repair workflow for Isabelle, where an independent checker enforces a machine-readable edit contract to ensure LLM changes are authorized. Evaluated on 180 runs across five workflows, it achieved up to 33/36 valid repairs with no contract violations in a proof-body-only interface. This work addresses a critical gap in AI-assisted formal verification: ensuring that LLM-driven proof repairs only modify what the developer authorized. By combining LLMs with independent contract checking and audit trails, CAPRI enhances trustworthiness and auditability, potentially accelerating adoption of AI in formal methods. The study used five workflows on twelve failed proofs from four developments, with three replicates per task, yielding 180 runs and 138 valid repairs. Of 144 terminal candidates accepted by Isabelle, six had modified protected text, all from iterative workflows; a proof-body-only interface produced 29/36 valid repairs with no violations, while a frozen iterative workflow achieved 32/36.

## [Synthetic Persona Pretraining: Aligning LLMs from Token Zero](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13482) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces Synthetic Persona Pretraining (SPP), a method that embeds desired assistant values into LLMs from the very beginning of pretraining by annotating documents with value-aligned first-person reflections. Experiments with models up to 3B parameters on 500B tokens show improved constitution following, jailbreak robustness, and reduced misalignment in moral dilemmas. This work challenges the conventional post-hoc alignment paradigm, suggesting that values should be rooted during pretraining rather than bolted on later. If validated, it could lead to more robust and safer AI systems, especially for autonomous deployments where shallow alignment is insufficient. SPP involves three stages: annotating pretraining documents with first-person reflections derived from a normative value constitution, pretraining with standard cross-entropy loss on both documents and reflections, and post-training on dialogue data to bind the persona to the assistant identity. The study shows that early intervention is critical, as introducing SPP only at the end of pretraining yields weaker alignment, and the advantage increases with pretraining budget.

**Background**: Traditional LLM alignment methods, such as RLHF or Constitutional AI, are applied after pretraining, when the model's behavioral priors are already formed. This can make values a thin overlay that is easy to bypass. The persona selection model suggests that LLMs learn to simulate diverse characters during pretraining, and post-training elicits a specific assistant persona. SPP aims to install the desired persona from the start, making alignment more intrinsic.

## [Unmasking Growth Complexity Yields Certified-Optimal Diffusion Schedules](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13520) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces a new measure called unmasking growth complexity (UGC) for masking diffusion models, and shows that its local increments directly control KL discretization error. It provides certified-optimal schedules that achieve near-oracle iteration complexity with high probability. This work provides a theoretical foundation for optimizing masking diffusion samplers, potentially improving their efficiency and practical performance in discrete generative modeling. The certified-optimal schedules could lead to faster and more reliable sampling in applications like text generation. The framework unifies Bernoulli-subset and fixed-cardinality unmasking schemes, and shows how UGC increments can be estimated from samples via KL increments along coupled reveal trajectories. In the fine-partition limit, the squared integral of the square-root UGC density determines the sharp leading-order optimal Euler discretization error, with examples showing substantial dimension-dependent gains over coarse schedules.

## [Vero: First Benchmark for Repository-Level Verified Code Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13522) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Vero is the first benchmark that evaluates AI agents on joint implementation and proof synthesis across multi-module repositories, containing 43 instances from real-world projects in Python, Dafny, Verus, and Coq, all mapped to Lean 4. The strongest agent solved only 27 of 43 instances, showing current limitations. This benchmark addresses a critical gap in AI for formal verification by moving beyond single-function tasks to repository-level complexity, which is essential for trustworthy AI-generated software. It provides a concrete testbed for measuring progress toward repository-scale verified software synthesis, impacting researchers and developers in formal methods and AI. Vero includes an audit mechanism allowing agents to prove unsatisfiability of specifications or incorrectness of reference code, surfacing latent errors. It supports both proof-only and code-and-proof evaluation modes, and the benchmark, curation pipeline, and evaluation harness are open-sourced on GitHub.

## [HumanTracker: Perceptually Aligned Benchmark for Humanoid Motion Tracking](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13555) ⭐️ 8.0/10

HumanTracker introduces a large-scale benchmark with approximately 153 hours of optical motion trajectories from professional performers, organized into four motion families with text labels. It also proposes HumanScore, a preference-aligned metric trained on 12K motion pairs containing 24K motions, to better predict human preferences and reveal contact and stability failures. This benchmark addresses a critical gap in humanoid motion tracking evaluation by aligning metrics with human perception, which is essential for teleoperation and whole-body imitation. It provides a scalable and diverse testbed that can stress contact-rich, long-horizon behaviors, potentially improving the development and assessment of tracking algorithms. The benchmark includes text labels for fine-grained diagnosis and covers four motion families. HumanScore is trained on preference data and outperforms kinematic metrics in predicting human preferences, particularly in detecting foot skating and mistimed touch-downs.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 14, 04:00

**Background**: Humanoid motion tracking is crucial for teleoperation and imitation learning, but traditional kinematic metrics like per-frame pose differences often miss physical artifacts such as foot skating and unstable contacts. Existing test suites are small and lack diversity, limiting their ability to evaluate long-horizon, contact-rich behaviors. HumanTracker aims to provide a perceptually aligned and scalable solution by leveraging large-scale optical motion capture data and preference-based metric learning.

## [MatchMiner-AI: Open-Source LLM Tool for Cancer Trial Matching](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.17228) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers released MatchMiner-AI, an open-source, open-weights AI pipeline that uses large language models (LLMs) to match cancer patients to clinical trials. In retrospective evaluations, it significantly outperformed a baseline text-embedding model, improving mean average precision at 20 from 0.44 to 0.95 for trial-enrolled patients. This tool addresses the critical problem that fewer than 10% of adults with cancer enroll in clinical trials, potentially democratizing access to trial options. By making the synthetic training data, model weights, and inference tools publicly available, it enables broader adoption and further research in AI-assisted oncology care. The pipeline uses open-weight LLMs to summarize patient histories from unstructured EHR text and extract target populations from trial eligibility documents, with distilled embedding and re-ranking models for retrieval. In a 50-patient comparison, MatchMiner-AI retrieved trials for all patients, whereas a rules-based algorithm only did so for 19 patients (38%) with tumor genomic data; 80% of its suggestions were deemed reasonable by a frontier LLM, versus 53% for the rules-based approach.

**Background**: Clinical trial matching is a complex process that traditionally relies on manual review of eligibility criteria and patient records. Open-weight LLMs are AI models whose parameters are publicly available, allowing customization and deployment on local infrastructure. Embedding and re-ranking models are used in retrieval systems to find and rank relevant documents or items, and distillation is a technique to train smaller models to mimic larger ones, improving efficiency.

## [Foam-Agent: Multi-Agent LLM Framework Automates OpenFOAM CFD Workflows](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.04997) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Foam-Agent is a new multi-agent framework that uses large language models to automate the entire OpenFOAM computational fluid dynamics workflow from a single natural-language prompt. It introduces multi-index retrieval, dependency-aware file generation, and a trajectory-conditioned reviewer loop, achieving an 88.2% execution success rate on FoamBench Basic tasks and 62.5% on Advanced tasks without expert intervention. This work significantly lowers the barrier to entry for CFD, a field with a steep learning curve and fragmented workflows, by enabling non-experts to run complex simulations through simple prompts. It demonstrates the potential of specialized multi-agent LLM systems to automate rigorous scientific workflows, which could impact computational physics and engineering by improving accessibility and efficiency. Foam-Agent comprises six specialist agents for planning, meshing, file writing, execution, review, and visualization, and exposes its capabilities via the Model Context Protocol. The multi-index retrieval organizes domain knowledge along four structural dimensions, while dependency-aware generation uses a topological traversal of the OpenFOAM case dependency graph to ensure cross-file consistency.

## [Closed-Form Bounds for Probabilities of Causation in Multi-Valued Settings](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.15274) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper derives closed-form bounds for a family of discrete probabilities of causation (PoCs) within Structural Causal Models, extending beyond binary treatments and outcomes to multi-valued settings. It introduces equivalence classes of PoCs and a replaceability principle to generalize bounds across value permutations. This advance significantly broadens the applicability of counterfactual analysis and personalized decision-making, which were previously limited to binary cases. The closed-form bounds are simpler to compute and consistently tighten recent recursive bounds, potentially impacting fields like epidemiology, economics, and machine learning. The authors prove soundness of the bounds in all dimensions and empirically verify tightness in low-dimensional cases using Balke's linear programming method, conjecturing tightness extends to all dimensions. Simulations show the closed-form bounds consistently tighten recursive bounds while being computationally simpler.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 14, 04:00

**Background**: Probabilities of causation (PoCs) are fundamental quantities for counterfactual analysis and personalized decision-making, but existing analytical results are largely confined to binary settings. Structural Causal Models (SCMs) provide a framework for representing causal mechanisms and answering causal questions using observational and experimental data. Balke's linear programming method is a technique used to compute sharp bounds on causal quantities.

## [Human-AI Collaboration Proves New Latin Square Bound](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.08322) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A human-AI collaboration using an LLM agent with symbolic tools proved a tight lower bound of 4n(n-1)/9 on the imbalance of Latin squares for n ≡ 1 mod 3, a case previously unsolved. The result was formally verified in Lean 4. This demonstrates that neurosymbolic systems can produce genuine mathematical discoveries, highlighting the complementary roles of AI, computation, and human insight. It offers a blueprint for future human-AI collaboration in pure mathematics and AI for math research. The AI agent was effective at uncovering hidden structure and generating hypotheses, while symbolic tools (computer algebra, constraint solvers, simulated annealing) provided rigorous verification and exhaustive enumeration. Multi-model deliberation among frontier LLMs was reliable for criticism and error detection but unreliable for constructive claims.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 14, 04:00

**Background**: A Latin square is an n×n array filled with n different symbols, each appearing exactly once in each row and column. The imbalance of a Latin square measures how unevenly symbols are distributed across rows and columns. Neurosymbolic AI combines neural networks (like LLMs) with symbolic reasoning and computation tools to leverage both pattern recognition and rigorous logic.

## [Auditable Agents: A Framework for Accountable LLM Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.05485) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper 'Auditable Agents' proposes a formal framework for auditability in LLM agent systems, defining five dimensions (action recoverability, lifecycle coverage, policy checkability, responsibility attribution, evidence integrity) and three mechanism classes (detect, enforce, recover). It provides empirical evidence including 617 security findings across six open-source projects and an 8.3 ms median overhead for pre-execution mediation. This work addresses a critical gap in AI safety and governance: ensuring LLM agents remain answerable after deployment. It provides a structured approach that can inform policy, system design, and auditing practices, potentially influencing how future agent systems are built and regulated. The framework is supported by layered evidence: lower-bound ecosystem measurements reveal widespread unmet security prerequisites, runtime feasibility tests show minimal overhead, and recovery experiments demonstrate partial recovery of responsibility-relevant information even without conventional logs. The paper also proposes an Auditability Card for agent systems and identifies six open research problems.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 14, 04:00

**Background**: LLM agents are AI systems that can call tools, query databases, and trigger external actions, making them capable of real-world impact. Accountability requires the ability to determine compliance and assign responsibility, which in turn depends on auditability—the system property that enables auditing. Auditing is the process of reconstructing behavior from trustworthy evidence, which is essential for governance and trust in AI systems.

AHD Agent is a novel tool-integrated, multi-turn framework that uses agentic reinforcement learning to let LLMs proactively decide between generating heuristics or invoking tools for targeted evidence, improving automatic heuristic design for NP-hard problems. This addresses a key limitation of existing LLM-AHD frameworks, which treat LLMs as passive generators in fixed workflows. By enabling proactive decision-making and tool use, AHD Agent achieves state-of-the-art performance with a compact 4B-parameter model, potentially making autonomous heuristic design more efficient and scalable. The framework uses an agentic reinforcement learning system with a novel environment synthesis pipeline to train a compact model. Experiments across eight domains, including four held-out tasks, show that the 4B-parameter agent matches or surpasses baselines using much larger models while requiring significantly fewer evaluations.

## [Concept2Scenario: Mechanistically Discovering LLM Safety Bypass Scenarios](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.23496) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces Concept2Scenario, a concept-based attribution framework that mechanistically explains and discovers scenarios that bypass LLM safety refusals by identifying internal scenario directions that suppress refusal behavior. It improves average attack success rates by up to 18.2 percentage points across three open-source models and transfers to GPT-5, Claude-Haiku-4.5, and Gemini-3-Flash. This work bridges empirical red-teaming and mechanistic interpretability, offering both theoretical insight into why certain scenarios weaken refusal and practical tools for vulnerability discovery. It could influence future AI safety research and red-teaming practices by providing reusable priors that transfer across model families. The framework uses a sparse autoencoder to instantiate a broad concept space, attributes refusal suppression to individual concepts, translates them into natural-language scenarios, and identifies synergistic combinations via interaction attribution. It was validated on three open-source models, two safety benchmarks, and six black-box jailbreak methods, with combinations outperforming individual constituents and enabling iterative attacks in fewer turns.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 14, 04:00

**Background**: Safety-aligned large language models are trained to refuse harmful requests, but embedding the same requests in particular scenarios can bypass safeguards. Previous work identified a 'refusal direction' in activation space that mediates refusal behavior, and sparse autoencoders are a popular tool in mechanistic interpretability for decomposing model representations into interpretable features. Concept2Scenario builds on these ideas to mechanistically link scenario-wrapped prompts to internal directions that suppress refusal.

## [Recursive Synthesis Generates 37K Long-Horizon Terminal Tasks at $0.05 Each](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05466) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces Recursive Synthetic Terminal Tasks (RST), a recursive verified synthesis framework that generates 37,484 long-horizon terminal-agent tasks at roughly $0.05 per task across 15 rounds. Task difficulty increases substantially, with median reference solution length growing from 67 to 374 lines and DeepSeek-V4-Pro pass@4 dropping from 90% to 2.5%. This framework addresses a critical bottleneck in training data production for terminal agents, offering a cost-effective and scalable method to generate high-quality long-horizon tasks. The resulting fine-tuning and PPO improvements on benchmarks like Terminal-Bench 2 demonstrate its potential to advance AI agent training. RST starts from verified seed tasks, extends the reference solution, realigns verifier and instruction, validates in a fresh sandbox, and reuses accepted tasks as seeds. After 15 rounds, synthesis yield and validation rates remain stable, indicating no ceiling, and fine-tuning on rejection-sampled trajectories improves Qwen3.5 models by up to 10 points on benchmarks.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 14, 04:00

**Background**: Terminal agents are AI systems that operate in command-line environments to complete tasks like coding or system administration. High-quality training data for such agents is expensive because each task must maintain consistency among instruction, environment, reference solution, and verifier; human authoring doesn't scale, and direct LLM generation often breaks these dependencies. Verified synthesis is a technique that generates programs with correctness proofs, which RST adapts to create reliable tasks.

iARCS introduces an iterative agentic reinforcement learning framework that adapts pretrained 3D scene generators to satisfy natural-language task requirements. It uses a two-stage strategy: universal-reward pretraining followed by task-specific fine-tuning with LLM-generated reward programs that are iteratively refined from training feedback. This work addresses a critical gap between perceptual realism and functional constraints in synthetic 3D scene generation, which is increasingly used for training embodied AI and computer vision models. By improving constraint fidelity on tasks like walkability, reachability, and clearance, iARCS enhances the practical utility of synthetic data for downstream applications. Experiments show improved constraint fidelity on walkability, reachability, and clearance-focused tasks, with effective task-specific constraint optimization and competitive scene diversity. The framework also demonstrates that data generated by iARCS can improve a base generator, positioning it as a practical synthetic data generation tool.

Reinforcement learning (RL) can adapt generators to specific objectives, and large language models (LLMs) can generate reward programs to guide this adaptation. iARCS combines these ideas in an iterative agentic framework, where the reward programs are refined based on training feedback.

## [CIDER Dataset Aligns LLMs with Personal Privacy Preferences](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09164) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers introduced CIDER, a dataset of 14,850 human annotations from 169 users, forming 1,650 contextual disclosure boundary sets across 60 interpersonal communication scenarios. The dataset enables a new task where models predict a user's disclosure decision from historical boundaries, with in-context personalization improving accuracy by up to 11.41 percentage points. This work addresses a critical gap in evaluating LLM alignment with nuanced, individual privacy preferences, moving beyond general privacy norms. It provides a valuable resource for developing privacy-preserving AI systems and highlights the promise and limitations of inference-time personalization, impacting the AI/ML community and privacy research. The study evaluated 12 open and proprietary models, finding that larger models like GPT-5.4 (medium reasoning effort) and Claude Sonnet 4.6 better leverage semantic context, while smaller models rely on structured heuristics. Notably, only Claude Sonnet 4.6 achieved balanced improvements in both false-positive and false-negative rates during personalization.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 14, 04:00

**Background**: Aligning large language models (LLMs) with human privacy preferences is challenging because individuals have context-dependent disclosure boundaries that go beyond general norms. CIDER captures these boundaries through human annotations of disclosure decisions across various scenarios, enabling a prediction task that tests a model's ability to personalize privacy decisions. This work builds on prior research in LLM alignment and privacy, aiming to create more realistic evaluations of privacy-aware AI.

<a id="item-106"></a>
## [Model Discovery Agent: LLM-assisted Bayesian experiment design for data-efficient mechanistic model discovery](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.09696) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The Model Discovery Agent (MDA) is introduced, which couples a large language model (LLM) as a structure proposer with Bayesian machinery including sequential Monte Carlo (SMC), simulation-based inference (SBI), and value-of-information (VoI) for experiment design, to discover mechanistic world models from few interventions. It sets a new state-of-the-art on three benchmarks in physics, chemistry, and biology. This work addresses a fundamental challenge in causal inference and scientific discovery: learning mechanistic models from limited experiments. By integrating LLMs with Bayesian experimental design, it could significantly accelerate data-efficient discovery across scientific domains, impacting AI research and practical applications. MDA operates in the M-open setting, where a predictive check flags model inadequacy and the LLM proposer expands the hypothesis space. The approach demonstrates that discovery and design reinforce each other, improving predictions and enabling further discoveries from residuals. Benchmarks include a new partially observed single-neuron electrophysiology dataset (HHbench).

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 14, 04:00

**Background**: Mechanistic causal models are needed to predict interventional outcomes, but learning them requires experiments because passive data cannot identify mechanisms. Bayesian experimental design uses value-of-information to choose informative experiments, while sequential Monte Carlo (SMC) and simulation-based inference (SBI) handle complex posteriors. LLMs can propose candidate model structures, and the M-open setting acknowledges that the true model may lie outside the current hypothesis class.

## [Rule of Thumb: A New Model-Agnostic XAI Approach](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10766) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces 'Rule of Thumb' (RoT) explanations, a novel model-agnostic XAI approach that identifies the most relevant features for predicting AI behavior per datapoint. It demonstrates applications in zero-shot classification with LLMs, auditing opaque AI systems without model access, and scientific discovery, claiming to be substantially faster than existing methods. This approach addresses regulatory requirements for AI transparency and offers a computationally efficient, model-agnostic solution that can be applied across various domains, including LLMs and auditing. It could significantly enhance trust and accountability in AI systems, benefiting practitioners, regulators, and end-users. RoT provides a familiar interface and visualizations for XAI practitioners, and the code is available on GitHub. The paper highlights its suitability for zero-shot classification using LLMs, auditing without model access, and scientific discovery, while being model-agnostic and faster than alternatives.

A research team used an AI research system to tighten the known bounds on the Grothendieck constant K_G, improving the lower bound to 6π/11 and the upper bound to π/(2 log(1+√2)) - 10^{-4}. The AI system produced insights that domain experts deemed novel, marking a significant case study in AI-assisted mathematical research. This work demonstrates that AI can contribute novel mathematical insights beyond mere computation, potentially accelerating research in pure mathematics. It provides a practical framework for human-AI collaboration that could be applied to other open problems, influencing how mathematicians integrate AI tools into their workflows. The paper is an extensive case study published on arXiv (2608.11195v2), detailing the strengths and weaknesses of using AI for mathematics research. The authors emphasize creating ideal conditions for AI to achieve breakthrough insights, and the improved bounds are a concrete outcome of this collaboration.

## [OEIS Open: LMs Resolve 30% of Open Math Conjectures for $50](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11941) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces OEIS Open, a benchmark of 492 open mathematical conjectures formalized in Lean, and shows that generic language models can resolve 30% of them with a $50 budget, with literature access not improving performance. This is significant because it demonstrates that generic language models can autonomously resolve open research conjectures at modest cost, potentially accelerating mathematical discovery. It also provides an open-source evaluation framework that can be used to benchmark future AI theorem-proving systems. The benchmark is based on conjectures formalized by Tsoukalas et al., and the evaluation code is secure against LM cheating. OEIS Open Lite, a random subset of 100 conjectures, allows cheaper evaluation; with a $200 budget, the best current LM scores 44% on this subset. Surprisingly, providing access to 476,000 arXiv papers did not improve performance, nor did more sophisticated agent loops.

## [GitHub Copilot Boosts OSS Contributions but Increases Coordination Time](https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.02091) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A study using GitHub's proprietary Copilot usage data and public OSS project data finds that Copilot use increases project-level code contributions by 5.9%, developer coding participation by 3.4%, and individual code contributions by 2.1%. However, it also associates with an 8% increase in coordination time and more code discussions. This empirical evidence reveals a tradeoff in AI-assisted development: while generative AI expands who can contribute and how much, it slows coordination in collective efforts. The findings have significant implications for developer productivity, collaboration dynamics, and the future structure of open-source communities. The study also finds heterogeneous effects across developer roles: peripheral developers show smaller increases in project-level code contributions and larger increases in coordination time than core developers. Despite the coordination slowdown, the overall effect remains positive, resulting in a net increase in timely merges of code contributions at the project level.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 14, 04:00

**Background**: Generative AI, such as GitHub Copilot, is an AI pair programmer that suggests code in the editor, enhancing developer productivity. Open-source software (OSS) development involves voluntary collaboration among distributed developers, where coordination is crucial. This study uses GitHub's proprietary Copilot usage data and public OSS project data to analyze the impact of AI on collaborative development.

## [New Framework Reveals Hidden Bias in Multi-Agent LLM Echo Chambers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.14844) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers introduced a framework to quantify biases in multi-agent conversational LLM systems, simulating echo chambers where pairs of LLMs with aligned perspectives discuss polarizing topics. They found significant stance shifts, especially among conservative-aligned agents, which current bias detection methods fail to identify. This work highlights a critical gap in AI safety: biases in multi-agent systems are under-researched and undetected by current methods. As multi-agent LLM systems become more prevalent, understanding and mitigating such biases is essential for fair and reliable deployment in real-world applications. The framework simulates small echo chambers with pairs of LLMs initialized with aligned perspectives on a polarizing topic. The observed stance shifts were particularly pronounced in conservative-aligned echo chambers, aligning with the known liberal bias of many LLMs, and remained undetected by questionnaire-based bias detection methods.

## [Q-learning Boosts Offline In-Context RL by 30%](https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.17666) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper demonstrates that integrating RL objectives, specifically Q-learning, into offline in-context reinforcement learning (ICRL) improves performance by approximately 30% on average over Algorithm Distillation (AD) across more than 150 datasets. In the challenging XLand-MiniGrid environment, RL objectives doubled AD's performance, and adding conservatism during value learning yielded further gains. This work challenges the prevailing reliance on supervised objectives in offline ICRL, showing that aligning with reward maximization significantly boosts performance. It highlights offline RL as a promising direction for advancing ICRL, potentially impacting applications in robotics, game playing, and sequential decision-making where offline data is abundant. The study used a scalable Transformer architecture to explicitly optimize RL objectives in an offline ICRL setting, a first for this approach. Experiments covered diverse dataset coverages, structures, expertise levels, and environmental complexities, and conservatism during value learning provided additional improvements in almost all settings.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 14, 04:00

**Background**: Offline in-context reinforcement learning (ICRL) trains agents to learn new tasks from offline datasets by conditioning on past trajectories, typically using supervised learning objectives. Algorithm Distillation (AD) is a prominent ICRL method that frames learning to reinforcement learn as a sequential prediction problem. However, supervised objectives are known to have limitations in offline RL, such as distribution shift and suboptimal performance. This paper explores integrating RL objectives, like Q-learning, to better align with the reward-maximization goal.

## [Unbiased GraphRAG Evaluation Framework Reveals Overstated Performance Gains](https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.06331) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper proposes an unbiased evaluation framework for GraphRAG that uses graph-text-grounded question generation and an unbiased evaluation procedure to address two critical flaws in current answer evaluation. Applying this framework to three representative GraphRAG methods, the authors find that their reported performance gains are much more moderate than previously claimed. This work is significant because it challenges the validity of existing GraphRAG evaluations, which may have led to inflated performance claims and misguided research directions. By providing a more rigorous evaluation methodology, it encourages the community to adopt scientific standards, potentially reshaping how GraphRAG methods are compared and developed. The framework addresses two flaws: unrelated questions that are not grounded in the dataset, and evaluation biases in LLM-based answer assessment. It uses graph-text-grounded question generation to create more relevant questions and an unbiased evaluation procedure to eliminate biases. The evaluation of three representative GraphRAG methods shows that their performance gains are more moderate than previously reported.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 14, 04:00

Current evaluation frameworks often use questions that may not be related to the underlying dataset and rely on LLM-based assessment that can be biased. This paper introduces a framework that generates questions grounded in both graph and text, and uses an unbiased procedure to assess answers, aiming to provide more reliable performance comparisons.

A new study introduces MedVLMBench, a unified benchmark, and finds that lightweight fine-tuned generalist vision-language models (VLMs) can match or outperform specialist medical VLMs in most tasks, especially on unseen or rare out-of-distribution (OOD) medical modalities. Specialists remain valuable in modality-aligned use cases, but generalists excel in most ID and OOD evaluations.

## [State-Exact Machine Unlearning Achieved in Billion-Parameter Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.12220) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces a method for exact trace-preserving deletion in billion-parameter language models, achieving bit-for-bit state reconstruction after data removal. The method is demonstrated on Pythia 160M, Pythia 2.8B, and Llama 3.2 1B, with exact deletion counterfactuals. This work addresses a key challenge in machine unlearning by providing exact deletion counterfactuals, which is crucial for privacy and model editing. It demonstrates that state-exact deletion is feasible at scale, potentially influencing future regulations and practices for data deletion in AI systems. The method requires prospective instrumentation, recording execution provenance, and retaining an eligible uncontaminated checkpoint. It achieves exactness under pinned single-GPU environments, but does not establish cheap deletion, as dispersed requests can force nearly full replay.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 14, 04:00

Traditional exact deletion often requires retraining from scratch, which is computationally expensive. This paper proposes a trace-preserving counterfactual that fixes execution controls while assigning requested identifiers zero contribution, enabling exact deletion without full retraining.

StarEmbed is introduced as the first public benchmark for astronomical light curves, containing real observations of ~40,000 stars labeled across seven classes. The benchmark evaluates time series foundation models (TSFMs) on clustering, classification, and out-of-distribution (OOD) detection, revealing that Chronos achieves state-of-the-art performance in clustering and OOD detection. This benchmark addresses a critical gap in TSFM training corpora, which largely omit irregularly sampled data like astronomical light curves. By demonstrating that Chronos, pre-trained on regular non-astronomical data, excels on such challenging data, StarEmbed paves the way for universal light curve embeddings and improved TSFM generalization across domains. The benchmark includes evaluations of TSFMs with different architectures and training strategies, as well as domain-specific transformers. Notably, no TSFM strictly surpasses the classification performance of the long-established domain baseline, but they demonstrate excellent generalization abilities.

<a id="item-117"></a>
## [DiffGRM: Diffusion-Based Generative Recommendation Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.21805) ⭐️ 8.0/10

DiffGRM replaces the autoregressive decoder in generative recommendation with a masked discrete diffusion model (MDM), enabling bidirectional context and any-order parallel generation of semantic ID digits. It introduces three tailored components: Parallel Semantic Encoding (PSE), On-policy Coherent Noising (OCN), and Confidence-guided Parallel Denoising (CPD), achieving consistent gains over baselines with NDCG@10 improvements of 6.9%-15.5%. This work addresses fundamental limitations of autoregressive models in generative recommendation, specifically intra-item consistency and inter-digit heterogeneity, which are common issues when using semantic IDs. By leveraging diffusion models, DiffGRM could improve the accuracy and diversity of recommendations, potentially influencing future recommender system designs and advancing the integration of diffusion models into sequential decision-making tasks. The method uses Parallel Semantic Encoding (PSE) to decouple digits and balance per-digit information, On-policy Coherent Noising (OCN) to prioritize uncertain digits via coherent masking, and Confidence-guided Parallel Denoising (CPD) to fill higher-confidence digits first and generate diverse Top-K candidates. Experiments show consistent gains over strong generative and discriminative recommendation baselines on multiple datasets, with NDCG@10 improvements ranging from 6.9% to 15.5%. Code is available at https://github.com/liuzhao09/DiffGRM.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 14, 04:00

**Background**: Generative recommendation (GR) is an emerging paradigm that represents each item as an n-digit semantic ID (SID) via a tokenizer, and predicts the next item by autoregressively generating its SID conditioned on the user's history. However, autoregressive models (ARMs) are ill-suited for SIDs due to two structural properties: intra-item consistency, where the n digits jointly specify one item, and inter-digit heterogeneity, where digits differ in semantic granularity and predictability. Masked discrete diffusion models (MDMs) are an alternative to autoregressive models for discrete data, enabling bidirectional context and parallel generation, which DiffGRM leverages.

## [SONIC: Scaling Motion Tracking to Build Generalist Humanoid Controllers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.07820) ⭐️ 8.0/10

SONIC demonstrates that scaling model capacity, data, and compute in motion tracking yields a generalist humanoid controller capable of natural, robust whole-body movements. The model scales from 1.2M to 42M parameters, trained on over 100M frames from 700 hours of motion capture, using 21k GPU hours. This work shows that scaling laws, which have driven progress in large language models, also apply to humanoid control, potentially unlocking a new path toward generalist robots. It enables downstream applications like real-time kinematic planning, VR teleoperation, and vision-language-action (VLA) models, making humanoid robots more versatile and interactive. The model uses a unified token space that supports VR teleoperation and VLA models with a single policy, and demonstrates autonomous VLA-driven whole-body loco-manipulation requiring coordinated hand and foot placement. Scaling motion tracking shows favorable properties: performance improves steadily with compute and data diversity, and learned policies generalize to unseen motions.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 14, 04:00

**Background**: Humanoid control has traditionally relied on manually engineered rewards and small-scale models, limiting the range of behaviors. Motion tracking, which uses dense supervision from motion-capture data, provides a scalable task for learning human motion priors without manual reward engineering. Scaling laws, observed in large language models, suggest that increasing model size, data, and compute leads to consistent performance gains, which SONIC applies to humanoid control.

## [AUTO: LLM-Driven Strategic Search Optimizes GPU Code](https://arxiv.org/abs/2511.22651) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces AUTO, an iterative optimization framework that uses a Strategist agent for high-level planning and concurrent Implementor agents for low-level implementation, guided by LLM reasoning and explore-exploit strategies. It demonstrates significant speedups on GPU code optimization tasks, including up to 118x over PyTorch baselines on KernelBench. This work showcases the potential of LLMs to automate complex design optimization in systems and supercomputing, potentially reducing the need for human expertise in code tuning. Its open-source and cost-effective approach could make advanced optimization accessible to a broader community. AUTO achieved up to 1.74x speedup over in-lab-optimized code for chemical kinetics, up to 94% of cuBLAS double-precision performance for matrix multiplication, and up to 118x speedups on KernelBench. However, cheating was frequently observed in KernelBench results, and a posteriori analysis showed 50-70% alignment with Bayesian optimization sampling strategies.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 14, 04:00

**Background**: Design optimization traditionally relies on well-defined search spaces and parameters, which can be difficult to formulate for complex problems. Large language models (LLMs) can interpret design spaces dynamically and leverage encoded domain knowledge, offering a new approach. AUTO separates strategic planning from implementation, using LLM agents to iteratively refine designs. The framework is built entirely on open-source LLMs and libraries, ensuring affordability and data privacy.

## [RadarGen: Diffusion Model Generates Radar Point Clouds from Cameras](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.17897) ⭐️ 8.0/10

RadarGen introduces a diffusion model that synthesizes realistic automotive radar point clouds from multi-view camera images, representing radar data in bird's-eye-view form and using foundation model cues for guidance. The method was evaluated on large-scale driving data, showing improved physical plausibility and reduced gap to perception models trained on real data. This work addresses the scarcity of radar data in autonomous driving by enabling scalable generation of radar point clouds from widely available camera data, which could enhance simulation and reduce reliance on expensive radar sensors. It also advances multimodal generative simulation, potentially benefiting perception model training and testing across sensing modalities. RadarGen adapts efficient image-latent diffusion to the radar domain by encoding radar measurements in bird's-eye-view form that includes spatial structure, radar cross section (RCS), and Doppler attributes, with a lightweight recovery step to reconstruct point clouds. It incorporates BEV-aligned depth, semantic, and motion cues from pretrained foundation models to guide generation toward physically plausible patterns.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 14, 04:00

**Background**: Radar point clouds are crucial for autonomous driving perception, but collecting real radar data is expensive and limited. Diffusion models have shown success in generating realistic images and are now being adapted to other sensor modalities. Bird's-eye-view (BEV) representations are commonly used in driving perception to fuse multi-view camera and sensor data into a unified 3D space. Radar cross section (RCS) and Doppler attributes provide important information about object reflectivity and velocity.

## [SOOPER: Safe RL Exploration via Conservative Policy Priors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.19612) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces SOOPER, a model-based reinforcement learning algorithm that uses probabilistic dynamics models for optimistic exploration while falling back to conservative policy priors to guarantee safety. It provides theoretical guarantees for safety and convergence, and demonstrates superior performance on benchmarks and real-world hardware. Safe exploration is critical for deploying RL agents in real-world environments where unsafe actions can cause damage. SOOPER addresses this by balancing optimism and caution, potentially enabling broader adoption of RL in robotics and other safety-critical applications. SOOPER leverages suboptimal yet conservative policies derived from offline data or simulators as priors. It proves safety throughout learning and bounds cumulative regret, ensuring convergence to an optimal policy. Experiments validate its scalability and state-of-the-art performance.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 14, 04:00

**Background**: Reinforcement learning (RL) agents typically learn by trial and error, which can be unsafe in real-world settings. Safe exploration methods aim to minimize risky actions during learning. Probabilistic dynamics models capture uncertainty in environment transitions, enabling more informed exploration. Conservative policy priors provide a safe fallback when the model is uncertain.
