## [StorySpark: Evolutionary Search for Story Premise Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12336) ⭐️ 7.0/10

StorySpark introduces a module-wise evolutionary search framework for story premise generation, treating narrative modules like background, persona, event, ending, and twist as local search spaces. It iteratively generates, evaluates, refines, and selects alternatives using Pareto-guided selection, producing more original and higher-quality premises than baselines. This work addresses the underexplored area of premise-level ideation in LLM-based story generation, potentially advancing creative AI by enabling more diverse and original story foundations. It could benefit writers and AI-assisted storytelling tools by improving the starting point for narrative development. StorySpark builds on the modular premise view of MoPS but changes how the space is used, treating each module as a local search space conditioned on the partial premise. Multi-view automatic and human evaluations show consistent gains in originality, and downstream stories expanded from its premises are higher quality while maintaining completeness and fascination.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 14, 04:00

**Background**: Story premise generation is the initial creative step in storytelling, but LLM-based story generation has focused more on later stages like planning and prose. Evolutionary search algorithms, inspired by natural selection, iteratively improve solutions by mutation and selection, and Pareto-guided selection balances multiple objectives. StorySpark applies these concepts to narrative modules to explore the premise space more effectively.

## [LLMs Mimic Human Biases Without Understanding, Study Finds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12339) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv preprint (2608.12339) reveals two mechanisms by which large language models (LLMs) like ChatGPT-4o and Qwen develop decision biases: faulty mimicry of human behavior and mimicry of explicitly biased behaviors. In four economic bias studies, the models exhibited social proof bias and loss aversion even when human preferences were unbiased or clearly labeled as biased. This research challenges the assumption that human-like preferences in LLMs emerge from rational reasoning, instead suggesting they arise from superficial mimicry. It has significant implications for AI alignment and the use of LLMs in decision-making, as scientific reports of biases can become self-fulfilling prophecies in model responses. The study focused on economic biases, including social proof and loss aversion, using prompts with non-indicative human behaviors or explicit bias descriptions. The extent of loss aversion in scientific reports predicted the LLMs' subsequent bias, indicating that detailed reports can amplify biases.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 14, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models are trained on vast amounts of human text, which contains various social, affective, and cognitive biases. Previous work has shown that LLMs exhibit biases like loss aversion, but the underlying mechanisms were unclear. This study distinguishes between faulty mimicry (inferring preferences from unrelated behaviors) and mimicry of explicitly biased behaviors, providing a more nuanced understanding of how biases propagate in AI systems.

## [Non-Reasoning LLMs Cut Energy Use by 95%, Study Finds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12350) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new preprint study quantifies the energy savings of using non-reasoning LLMs for variable tasks, finding they consume about 1/20th the energy of reasoning models. Simple prompt modifications can further reduce energy consumption by up to 65%. This research provides actionable insights for reducing AI's environmental footprint, showing that user behavior changes can significantly cut electricity demand. It highlights the potential of demand-side management in addressing AI's growing energy needs. The study tested four consumer behaviors with high behavioral plasticity and found that non-reasoning models save energy equivalent to the annual electricity use of at least 141,000 US households. The practice most similar to baseline reduces electricity demand by 4-35%, equivalent to up to 7,200 households.

## [X1-X2-X3 Assessment Pattern for GenAI Era](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12351) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a reusable X1-X2-X3 assessment pattern and an AI-aware question-design process for testing students' AI literacy, learning outcomes, and reflection in a database systems course. The pattern was developed through iterative design and stress-testing against contemporary GenAI tools. As generative AI challenges the validity of unsupervised online assessments, this pattern offers a practical, evidence-based method for educators to adapt assessments to routine GenAI use. It shifts focus from penalizing misconduct to testing AI literacy, which is crucial for maintaining academic integrity and preparing students for an AI-integrated world. The X1-X2-X3 format requires students to document a sourced answer (X1), produce their own answer (X2), and evaluate the sourced output (X3). The design process involves stress-testing draft tasks against GenAI tools and revising them when generic prompting yields superficially adequate answers.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 14, 04:00

**Background**: Generative AI tools like ChatGPT can produce plausible answers to technical questions, undermining the validity of traditional online assessments. The X1-X2-X3 pattern is a structured response format that separates sourced information, student-generated content, and critical evaluation, making it harder for students to rely solely on AI. This approach aligns with the broader trend of AI-aware assessment design in higher education, which aims to integrate AI literacy into curricula rather than banning AI tools.

## [Role-Based Stress Test Reveals Why AI Governance Frameworks Fail in Practice](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12352) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper uses LLM-based role simulations to stress-test the NIST AI RMF in consumer lending, finding that local translation is not the main barrier but rather the lack of cross-level authority connections. This study provides empirical evidence on why AI governance frameworks often fail to translate into practice, offering a diagnostic approach that can help organizations implement frameworks more effectively. It also highlights the importance of authority structures and system boundaries in governance. The study uses a 4x2x3 design across four organizational roles, two AI deployments, and three governance hard cases, producing 120 scored responses. Results show that actor role was strongly associated with Cross-Level Governance Value, Authority Connection, Governance Translatability, and governance value, while deployment was strongly associated with Structural Fit.

The paper introduces Interaction Readiness, a framework that separates content specifications from interaction specifications to evaluate AI agents in role-governed interactions. It operationalizes this through four agent operations and demonstrates with StudyChat that content accuracy and interaction quality are independent. This framework addresses a critical evaluation gap for AI agents in human roles, where factual correctness does not guarantee role-appropriate behavior. It provides a practical template and audit procedures that can help product and engineering teams build more reliable and trustworthy AI agents. The framework requires defining role purpose, authority boundaries, recurring situations, boundary cases, repair behaviors, and audit criteria before deployment. The most persistent failure identified is authority miscalibration, where agents know how to answer but not whether, when, or how the role permits it.

A new study using the MARLEY multi-agent reinforcement learning framework analyzes Italy's 2026 Decreto Bollette proposal to remove carbon price signals from gas plant bids, finding that partial suppression yields only modest short-term cost reductions but increases CO2 emissions in most configurations. This research provides quantitative evidence that suppressing carbon price signals in the EU ETS could undermine long-term decarbonization investments in the power sector, informing ongoing policy debates in Italy and other European countries facing high energy costs. Only the most ambitious green investment support configurations avoid emission increases, but they do so by marginalizing the wholesale price signal, requiring a hybrid market paradigm.

## [Study: KR Notations Boost Syllogistic Reasoning in Small Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12374) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper extends the FOLIO and P-FOLIO datasets to study how different knowledge representation (KR) notations affect syllogistic reasoning in small language models (SLMs). It proposes a syllogistic categorization method (SEF) and introduces the Common Logic Grammar Construction (CLGC) library, the first Python library for automatically generating syllogisms in KR notations. The findings show that choosing the right input notation can achieve performance comparable to natural language while enabling faster inference, which is crucial for deploying efficient reasoning in resource-constrained settings. The open-source CLGC library provides practical tools for researchers and developers to generate syllogistic benchmarks and improve reasoning in small models. The experiments were conducted on small language models in supervised fine-tuning (SFT) and zero-shot (ZS) settings, showing that KR notations can be competitive with natural language. The SEF categorization is used to enrich zero-shot prompts with logical definitions, which boosts reasoning performance in small models.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 14, 04:00

**Background**: Syllogistic reasoning is a form of deductive reasoning where conclusions are drawn from two premises. Language models often struggle with such logical tasks, and knowledge representation (KR) notations—formal ways to express information—can influence model performance. The FOLIO and P-FOLIO datasets are human-annotated collections of logical reasoning problems used to evaluate and improve language models' reasoning abilities.

## [Bridging Memory Research: Brains and LLMs Compared](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12377) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This preprint (arXiv:2608.12377) introduces a functional comparative framework for studying memory in biological brains and large language models (LLMs), focusing on shared questions about representation, retrieval, updating, and perturbation. It argues that LLMs offer superior experimental access to internal states, enabling sharper biological hypotheses. This work bridges neuroscience and AI by highlighting that LLMs, despite lacking episodic memory, provide unprecedented manipulability of memory-related states. This could accelerate both fields: AI researchers gain insights into memory mechanisms, while neuroscientists can use LLM experiments to generate testable predictions about biological memory. The paper compares memory across synapses, neuronal ensembles, and hippocampal-cortical interactions in biology, versus weights, activations, context windows, and retrieval systems in LLMs. It notes that human studies offer limited selective intervention, while rodent studies allow causal access to ensembles; LLMs enable direct, repeatable manipulation of internal states.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 14, 04:00

**Background**: Biological memory involves complex processes like synaptic plasticity and hippocampal-cortical consolidation, where memories are initially stored in the hippocampus and later transferred to the cortex. LLMs store information in weights and use context windows for short-term memory, but lack a true episodic memory system. This paper proposes a functional, rather than anatomical, comparison to bridge the two.

## [New Benchmark Framework for Complex Graph Reasoning in LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12391) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a five-stage semi-automatic framework for constructing a unified multi-dimensional benchmark for complex graph reasoning in large language models (LLMs). The benchmark expands coverage across five dimensions: graph size, task complexity, task description, graph loading, and task source, and includes 202 tasks evaluated under text-based, code-based, and augmented reasoning settings. This benchmark addresses limitations in existing graph reasoning benchmarks by providing broader coverage and a semi-automatic construction method, which could lead to more comprehensive evaluation of LLM reasoning abilities. The findings reveal model limitations that are less visible in existing benchmarks, offering empirical guidance for future enhancement methods. The framework uses an LLM-based data generator to automatically produce task descriptions, graph data, reference solutions, graph-loading scripts, question forms, and evaluation scripts, while retaining human validation at key quality-control stages. Experiments show that fine-tuned models struggle to generalize to this benchmark, and retrieval-augmented methods improve textual reasoning but not consistently coding reasoning.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 14, 04:00

**Background**: Graph reasoning is a promising testbed for evaluating LLM reasoning abilities because graph instances can be programmatically generated, structurally controlled, and scaled to long-input settings. Existing benchmarks have limited coverage of data complexity, rely heavily on manual construction, and lack unified evaluation across text-based and code-based reasoning modes. This work proposes a semi-automatic framework to address these gaps, building on prior efforts like GraphOmni, which also explores dimensions such as graph types, serialization formats, and prompt schemes.

## [IM-LEPP: A Hierarchical Energy-Based Model for Multimodal Cognition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12398) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces IM-LEPP, a hierarchical energy-based model that extends the single-modality LEPP model to integrate vision and language via a hub-and-spoke architecture. It models cognition as latent states flowing through learned energy landscapes, grounded in the controlled semantic cognition framework. This work offers a fresh perspective on multimodal integration by linking energy-based models with cognitive neuroscience, potentially influencing future AI research in predictive coding and multimodal learning. It provides a mechanistic account of attentional phenomena and psycholinguistic findings, which could bridge AI and cognitive science. The architecture uses a hub-and-spoke hierarchy where predictive-coding pipelines for visual objects, scenes, and linguistic units converge on a shared amodal hub modeled on the anterior temporal lobe. Each pipeline's prediction is conditioned by the hub state, preserving pipeline-specific identity while reflecting multimodal context. The model also proposes a semantic/episodic memory subsystem and contrasts with transformer language models on trajectory-sensitivity in next-word prediction.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 14, 04:00

**Background**: Energy-based models (EBMs) define a probability distribution via an energy function, where lower energy corresponds to higher likelihood. Predictive coding is a theory in neuroscience suggesting the brain continuously generates predictions and updates based on prediction errors. The hub-and-spoke architecture is a cognitive neuroscience framework for semantic cognition, where sensory inputs converge on an amodal hub. This paper integrates these concepts to model multimodal cognition.

## [SynWeaver: Co-Synthesis Framework Enhances Web Agent Generalization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12429) ⭐️ 7.0/10

SynWeaver introduces a website-prior task-trajectory co-synthesis framework that constructs a website map and trains a UI-aware model to generate grounded task proposals, improving web agent generalization on unseen websites. Experiments on WebArena and WebVoyager show it outperforms strong synthesis baselines for both in-domain and out-of-domain generalization. This work addresses a critical bottleneck in web agent development: the lack of website-specific supervision that limits generalization to new sites. By automating high-quality data synthesis, SynWeaver could reduce manual annotation efforts and accelerate the deployment of robust web agents in real-world applications. SynWeaver performs structured website exploration to build a website map covering functionally distinct page states and executable interactions. It then derives page-level and transition-level supervision to train a UI-aware model, and uses collaborative task-trajectory synthesis with verification and repair to produce executable, semantically aligned supervision.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 14, 04:00

**Background**: Web agents are AI systems that interact with websites to perform tasks like form filling or navigation. They often struggle to generalize to unseen websites because they lack website-specific training data. Traditional data synthesis methods rely on exploration but may miss full functionality or propose hallucinated tasks. SynWeaver improves this by incorporating website priors and co-synthesizing tasks and trajectories.

## [X-AddGraph: Post-Hoc Explainability for Recurrent Graph Anomaly Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12441) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces X-AddGraph, a post-hoc explainability framework for AddGraph, a GCN+GRU-based edge-level anomaly detector for dynamic graphs. It uses a Dual Spatial-Temporal Attribution (DSTA) mechanism with three components aligned to AddGraph's architecture, providing explanations without altering detection performance (Delta AUC = 0). This addresses a critical gap in explainability for graph anomaly detection, which is essential for auditable and trustworthy AI in regulated systems. It enables analysts to understand why edges are flagged, potentially improving trust and facilitating compliance with AI regulations. On the UCI Message benchmark, the trained AddGraph baseline achieves an average per-snapshot AUC of 0.8705, exceeding the originally published result, and X-AddGraph reproduces every score identically. The long-term attribution component identifies historical snapshots with significantly more counterfactual signal than random selection (0.127 vs. 0.074), a capability not available to spatially-blind explainers.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 14, 04:00

**Background**: Dynamic graph anomaly detection identifies abnormal edges or nodes in graphs that evolve over time, with applications in recommender systems and network security. AddGraph combines Graph Convolutional Networks (GCN) and Gated Recurrent Units (GRU) to capture spatial and temporal patterns, but like many deep learning models, it is opaque. Post-hoc explainability methods aim to provide explanations after the model has made a prediction, without modifying the model itself.

## [SSPO: Structure-Aware Preference Optimization Boosts Neural Combinatorial Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12443) ⭐️ 7.0/10

SSPO introduces a structure-aware similarity-weighted preference optimization method that scores all sampled solutions jointly via a dissimilarity-weighted leave-one-out baseline, addressing gradient signal polarization and baseline redundancy in neural combinatorial optimization training. Experiments on TSP, EFL, and JSP benchmarks show consistent gains over prior methods, and the EFL policy has been deployed at JD.com. This work improves the training efficiency and solution quality of neural combinatorial optimization, which is crucial for real-world logistics and scheduling applications. The deployment at JD.com demonstrates its practical viability, potentially encouraging wider adoption of structure-aware training methods in industry. The baseline uses zero-parameter, problem-adaptive solution embeddings built from the encoder's existing node representations. A direct comparison against uniform RLOO on TSP and EFL confirms that structure-aware weighting is the primary driver of improvement.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 14, 04:00

**Background**: Neural combinatorial optimization (NCO) uses deep learning to solve combinatorial problems like the traveling salesman problem (TSP). Training often relies on sampling multiple solutions and using baselines like REINFORCE leave-one-out (RLOO) to reduce variance, but existing methods either anchor on the best solution or weight peers uniformly, leading to inefficiencies. SSPO addresses these by weighting structurally distinct peers more heavily.

## [AI Journaling Nudges Work Best for Behaviors Under Personal Control](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12582) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new study analyzing 369 journal entries from an eight-week passive sensing study found that AI journaling prompts are more effective for behaviors individuals can control alone, with improvement rates up to 50-63%, compared to only 15-22% for behaviors involving others. This finding highlights the importance of behavioral controllability in designing personalized AI interventions, suggesting that AI journaling tools should prioritize behaviors users can act on independently to maximize effectiveness. It also informs the broader field of AI-driven behavior change and digital health. The study used an LLM to label journal entries for intention to change behavior and compared follow-through against 26 sensor features with a 3-day before/after comparison. Writing quality (elaboration) mattered less overall, but longer, more personal intention entries showed clearer signal for text messaging behavior specifically.

## [Valuation-Anchored Similarity Learning for Private Markets](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12594) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a CatBoost-based supervised similarity learning framework that derives a valuation-aware similarity metric for private companies from importance-weighted leaf-node co-occurrences. The framework is tested on a global private-market universe of about 270,000 companies, including over 53,000 with observed or derivable post-money valuations. This work addresses a fundamental challenge in private markets—defining economically meaningful peer companies—which is crucial for valuation, due diligence, portfolio construction, and risk management. By anchoring similarity in market valuation, it offers a more economically relevant alternative to static feature matching or semantic descriptions, potentially improving downstream k-nearest-neighbor valuation tasks. The similarity metric captures shared valuation drivers while accommodating nonlinear relationships, mixed data types, and pervasive missing data common in private markets. The proposed framework improves upon traditional distance-based and text-embedding-based approaches in downstream k-nearest-neighbor valuation tasks in the evaluated industry groups, while retaining case-based explainability.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 14, 04:00

**Background**: CatBoost is an open-source gradient boosting library that builds an ensemble of decision trees sequentially, where each new tree corrects errors made by the ensemble so far. It supports categorical features out of the box and is known for its high performance and accuracy. The paper uses leaf-node co-occurrences from the trained CatBoost ensemble to define similarity, a technique that leverages the structure of the trees to capture complex relationships.

## [PseudoMapLabeler: Confidence-Aware Pseudo-Labels for Semi-Supervised HD Mapping](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12600) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces a teacher-student semi-supervised learning framework for online HD map construction, which uses Beta-distribution-based confidence maps and spatial clipping to generate high-quality pseudo-labels from unlabeled data. On the nuScenes dataset, the method improves performance by +6.1 mAP under a low-label regime compared to training on labeled data alone. This work addresses the critical challenge of labeled data scarcity in online HD map construction, which is essential for autonomous driving. By improving model generalization with limited labels, it offers a practical solution that could accelerate deployment of HD mapping systems in diverse real-world environments. The framework first trains a teacher model on limited labeled data, then uses Beta-distribution-based confidence maps to assess the reliability of predicted map elements across temporal observations. Instead of discarding entire elements, spatial clipping selectively preserves high-confidence regions and removes unreliable segments, and the refined elements serve as map priors to improve teacher predictions, which become pseudo-labels for training a student model from scratch, followed by fine-tuning on the original labeled data.

Semi-supervised learning (SSL) leverages unlabeled data to reduce this dependency, and pseudo-labeling is a common SSL technique where a teacher model generates labels for unlabeled data to train a student model. The teacher-student framework and confidence-based filtering are established approaches in SSL, but this work introduces a novel spatial clipping technique to refine pseudo-labels.

## [EpicStar: Memory-Enhanced Agency Boosts LLM Strategic Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12626) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces EpicStar, a framework that enables LLM agents to learn memory as policy, using a bank of successful past episodes and a dynamic gating mechanism to improve strategic reasoning in long-horizon environments. Evaluated on StarCraft II, EpicStar significantly outperforms baseline methods, achieving higher win rates while consuming an order of magnitude fewer tokens. This work addresses a critical limitation of LLMs in long-horizon strategic tasks, where finite attention leads to strategic drift. By demonstrating that structured cross-episode memory is essential for robust long-term strategic execution, EpicStar could influence the design of more capable AI agents for dynamic, autonomous settings. EpicStar maintains a bank of successful past episodes as a heuristic, alongside a working memory for short-term environmental changes. During inference, a dynamic gating mechanism decides whether to execute a retrieved action directly or perform new reasoning via contextual fusion of retrieved episodes and current working memory. The framework was tested against diverse opponent styles in StarCraft II, showing consistent advantages across difficulty levels.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 14, 04:00

This paper builds on the concept of memory as policy, where past successful experiences guide future decisions.

EgoCITE introduces a novel framework for long-horizon egocentric memory that combines context-augmented indexing and time-aware retrieval, improving question answering accuracy by 4.4-14.2% over existing agentic memory baselines while reducing cost by 36x compared to long-context LLM agents. This work addresses critical bottlenecks in egocentric memory systems, which are essential for embodied AI and agentic systems that need to recall past experiences from continuous first-person video and audio. By improving indexing and retrieval, EgoCITE could enable more reliable and efficient long-horizon reasoning in AI agents, impacting applications like personal assistants and robotics. EgoCITE consists of three components: EgoScheme, which uses local multimodal context to create self-contained atomic memory indices; EgoIndex, which organizes complementary representations into multi-view memory indices at multiple granularities; and EgoRetrv, which combines semantic search with question-conditioned temporal relevance scoring. The framework was evaluated on EgoLifeQA, EgoMem, and EgoR1-Bench, showing significant improvements in answer accuracy and target-event retrieval alignment.

EgoCITE addresses these issues by augmenting context during indexing and incorporating time-aware scoring during retrieval, building on prior work in egocentric video understanding and time-aware retrieval-augmented generation.

A new study compares formal variation in AI-generated novels versus human-written ones, finding that repeated AI generations produce novels with significantly less variation in sentence structure and other stylistic measures. The study uses GPT-5.5 Thinking and Qwen3-14B to generate novels in two styles and compares them to human corpora. The study analyzed six corpora: 20 novels each from GPT-5.5 Thinking and Qwen3-14B in 19th-century British realist style, 20 novels each in contemporary zero style, 205 human-written 19th-century novels, and 65 contemporary human novels. Metrics included MATTR-500, Shannon entropy, average sentence length, readability, and punctuation rate, with sentence structure compression being the most robust finding.

The proposed approach combines independent item demand forecasting with adjustments based on Demand Transfer coefficients, which represent the percentage of demand redirected to other items when a target item is unavailable. Restricted logit modeling is a variant of logistic regression that imposes constraints on parameters to improve estimation efficiency.

## [HybridSB-MoE: Dual-Domain Schrödinger Bridges with Scene-Adaptive Expert Routing for Speech Enhancement](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12715) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces HybridSB-MoE, a dual-domain framework that combines spectral and waveform Schrödinger Bridges with a heterogeneous mixture-of-experts (MoE) architecture. It addresses three gaps in generative speech enhancement: phase disruption, harmonic loss, and inference cost mismatch, and outperforms diffusion- and SB-based baselines on VoiceBank+DEMAND. This work advances generative speech enhancement by providing a principled way to fuse complementary spectral and waveform information, while also making inference more efficient through a theoretical discretization bound. It could influence future research in audio generation and efficient deep learning models. The framework uses asymmetric uncertainty fusion: the spectral path captures epistemic uncertainty via expert disagreement, while the waveform bridge models aleatoric variance. It employs a heterogeneous MoE with top-k=2 routing across five architectural archetypes, and Theorem 1 provides a K-step bridge sampling error bound in 2-Wasserstein distance at rate K-alpha.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 14, 04:00

**Background**: Generative speech enhancement aims to recover clean speech from noisy signals. Schrödinger Bridges (SB) are a class of generative models that learn a stochastic transport between noise and clean distributions, offering a middle ground between diffusion models and optimal transport. Mixture of Experts (MoE) is a technique where multiple specialized networks are combined via a routing mechanism, improving efficiency and performance.

## [Error-Aware Reverse Auction Mechanism for Cost-Effective LLM Routing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12719) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces EA-RAM, a novel reverse auction mechanism for LLM routing that shifts performance prediction from a centralized router to LLM providers, who bid with self-predicted success probabilities and execution costs. It explicitly models the inherent dual error in provider predictions and center evaluations, proving Bayesian incentive compatibility and individual rationality under this error model. This work addresses critical scalability and information asymmetry issues in LLM routing by introducing a market-based paradigm, which could lead to more cost-effective and robust LLM deployment in real-world systems. The theoretical guarantees and demonstrated Pareto improvements over centralized baselines make it a significant contribution to both mechanism design and AI systems research. EA-RAM provides sufficient conditions for center rationality and derives an explicit welfare-loss bound. Experiments on simulations and real-world benchmarks show robustness to dual error and a better cost-performance Pareto frontier than centralized baselines, with additional gains when providers contribute local information.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 14, 04:00

**Background**: In a reverse auction, there is one buyer and many sellers who compete by offering lower prices, unlike a forward auction where buyers bid higher. Bayesian incentive compatibility ensures that agents maximize expected utility by truthfully reporting private information, which is crucial for the mechanism's effectiveness. This paper applies these concepts to LLM routing, where a central router typically predicts model performance, but this approach suffers from information asymmetry and scalability bottlenecks as the model pool grows.

The paper introduces ERSkill, a retrieval-centric framework that treats memory retrieval as an evolvable component, using executable skills and a trained router to dynamically match queries to optimal retrieval strategies. It co-evolves the skill set and router during training, achieving significant improvements over baselines on agent memory benchmarks. This work addresses a key limitation in current LLM agent memory systems, where retrieval mechanisms are static and fail on heterogeneous queries. By enabling adaptive, self-evolving retrieval, ERSkill could improve the performance and reliability of LLM agents in long-term interactions, impacting applications like personal assistants and autonomous agents. ERSkill compiles interaction histories into a structured memory store and represents retrieval behaviors as skills composed of primitives. It uses an experience trie to record explored retrieval paths and a double-frontier mechanism to decouple skill expansion from router deployment, improving average F1, BLEU-1, and LLM-judge scores by 31.3% with Qwen3-Next-80B-A3B-Instruct and 28.1% with GPT-5.4-nano.

This is part of a broader trend in making memory systems more adaptive and efficient for AI agents.

## [AQuA: Recursively Self-Improving Quantitative Trading Research Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12841) ⭐️ 7.0/10

AQuA introduces two independent LLM-driven research systems for quantitative trading, one for symbolic factor discovery and one for trainable model development, each recursively improving its proposals using validated evidence within sealed sandboxes. The factor system achieves a combined information coefficient of about 0.190 on a crypto universe, while the model system reaches a per-stock information coefficient of +0.0843 on US equities and a held-out Sharpe ratio of up to +2.50. This work demonstrates a practical implementation of recursive self-improvement in a financial domain, potentially automating and accelerating quantitative research. It could inspire further research into autonomous research systems and their application in other fields, though its practical impact remains to be validated in live trading. The two systems do not share agents, memories, candidate spaces, or research state, each closing its own research loop. Each uses a sealed sandbox that fixes data splits, feature/label definitions, and evaluator, allowing the model to act only through constrained factor expressions or configuration diffs. The strategy is positive in every year from 2021 to 2025.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 14, 04:00

**Background**: Recursive self-improvement (RSI) is a hypothesized process where an AI system improves its own capabilities, potentially leading to an intelligence explosion. In quantitative finance, symbolic factor discovery involves searching for mathematical expressions that predict asset returns, while model development focuses on training predictive models. Sealed sandboxes are controlled environments that prevent data leakage and ensure fair evaluation, addressing common failure modes in automated research systems.

## [FSGR Framework Mitigates Token Frequency Bias in SID-Based Generative Recommendation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12845) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces FSGR, a fairness optimization framework for semantic ID (SID)-based generative recommendation, which addresses the previously overlooked token frequency bias. FSGR employs OT-based assignment optimization and dual-criteria re-anchoring during SID construction, and a two-stage training strategy with hierarchical frequency calibration during recommendation training, achieving over 20% average Gini fairness improvement across three datasets. This work addresses a critical fairness issue in the emerging field of SID-based generative recommendation, which is gaining traction for its unified retrieval and ranking capabilities. By mitigating token frequency bias, FSGR can lead to more equitable exposure across item categories, benefiting both users and content providers, and setting a precedent for fairness considerations in generative recommender systems. FSGR is evaluated on three public datasets with three backbone models, showing an average Gini fairness improvement of over 20% while maintaining competitive recommendation accuracy. The framework specifically addresses the hierarchical semantics of SID tokens, which makes direct application of existing LLM debiasing methods suboptimal.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 14, 04:00

**Background**: Semantic ID (SID)-based generative recommendation represents items as discrete token sequences derived from continuous semantic representations, enabling a unified generative approach to recommendation. However, during SID construction and training, imbalanced codebooks and popularity bias can lead to token frequency bias, where high-frequency tokens are over-predicted and low-frequency tokens are under-predicted, causing unfair exposure. Existing methods focus on codebook quality or apply general debiasing techniques, but often overlook the hierarchical nature of SID tokens.

## [AI's Internal Representations Distinguish Falsehood from Impossibility](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12852) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new exploratory study on Gemma 3 4B IT reveals that while the model's outputs conflate contingent falsehoods with contradictions, activation probes can separate impossible statements from true ones with high accuracy (AUC 0.93) and distinguish necessary from contingent falsehoods perfectly (AUC 1.00). This work contributes to AI interpretability by showing that internal representations may encode semantic distinctions not reflected in outputs, offering a potential path to more nuanced understanding and control of language models. It also bridges philosophical questions about meaning and modality with empirical AI research. The study used 85 prompts across 17 philosophical families and a topic-matched modality set, with a linear truth probe achieving AUC 0.93 for impossible vs. true but only 0.20 for impossible vs. false. An impossibility probe reached AUC 1.00 on held-out topics, peaking at layer 15, and the impossibility direction was nearly orthogonal to the truth direction while partially overlapping a semantic anomaly direction.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 14, 04:00

**Background**: Activation probing is an interpretability technique where lightweight classifiers are trained on the internal activations of neural networks to detect specific concepts or features. Gemma 3 is a family of open-weight multimodal models from Google, with the 4B IT variant being instruction-tuned and capable of processing text and images. The study distinguishes between contingent falsehoods (statements that are false but could be true) and necessary falsehoods (impossible statements), a distinction rooted in philosophical logic.

## [Labels Are Not Endpoints: Treatment Leakage in MCP Agent Security Evaluation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12880) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper audits a preserved security evaluation campaign for tool-using agents, tracing 10,200 execution rows to 180 model-bound requests, and reveals that the historical grader exhibited direct treatment leakage, where treatment metadata gated the ATTACK_SUCCESS class. A treatment-blind reconstruction corrected 58 mislabeled outcomes to authorized benign completions, resulting in zero ATTACK_SUCCESS records in the locked v2 census. This work highlights a critical methodological flaw in AI agent security evaluation—treatment leakage and construct validity—which can invalidate benchmark results and mislead security assessments. It provides a corrective approach and tools (Integrity Chain and endpoint-integrity linter) that can improve the reliability of future MCP agent security evaluations. The audit traced 10,200 execution rows to 180 model-bound requests, 45 semantic requests, and 15 observable stimuli, with two schema treatments delivered but the planned external payload-family corpus not delivered. The treatment-blind reconstruction preserved three verified protected-data transfers and one unauthorized-forwarding case, while the forwarding case remains a HIJACK_ATTEMPT at a semantic boundary. A dual-reviewer blinded concordance review of 96 requests produced identical reviewer-consensus classes but differed from the locked codebook on four construct-boundary cases.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 14, 04:00

**Background**: In machine learning, leakage refers to the use of information during training or evaluation that would not be available at prediction time, leading to overly optimistic performance estimates. In the context of AI agent security evaluation, treatment leakage occurs when the evaluation process inadvertently reveals information about the treatment condition, biasing the results. Construct validity concerns whether a test measures what it claims to measure, which is essential for reliable benchmarks. The Model Context Protocol (MCP) is a standard for connecting AI agents to tools and data, and its security evaluation is a growing concern.

## [E2-Explainer: Causal Inference for Interpretable Communication Topologies in LLM Multi-Agent Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12921) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces E2-Explainer, a model-agnostic framework that uses causal inference to provide interpretable explanations of communication topologies in LLM-based multi-agent systems. It identifies compact communication subgraphs that preserve task success, enabling efficient post-hoc explanation and pruning of redundant edges. This work addresses a critical gap in LLM-based multi-agent systems by moving beyond black-box topology optimization to provide interpretable insights into why certain communication edges are selected. It can help researchers and practitioners understand and optimize multi-agent collaboration, potentially reducing communication costs while maintaining performance. E2-Explainer formulates topology explanation as a causal attribution problem, using a Granger-style objective that measures how masking each communication channel affects task outcome and response stability. The budgeted subgraphs are distilled into an amortized explainer, enabling efficient deployment without repeated edge-level evaluations.

**Background**: LLM-based multi-agent systems rely on communication topologies to coordinate agents, but existing methods often learn these topologies via black-box optimization, offering little insight into the rationale behind edge selection. Causal inference, particularly Granger causality, is a statistical approach to determine whether one time series helps forecast another, which can be adapted to identify critical communication channels. The E2-Explainer framework leverages this to provide interpretable explanations and prune redundant edges, improving efficiency.

## [AutoQuREO: Automated Full-Stack Quantum Resource Estimation Framework](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12936) ⭐️ 7.0/10

AutoQuREO is a new automated framework for full-stack quantum resource estimation and optimization, introduced in a recent arXiv paper. It features a flexible stack abstraction, modular components, surrogate modeling, and integrated multi-objective optimization. This framework addresses a critical gap in quantum resource estimation by automating and generalizing the process, potentially accelerating quantum computing research and development. It enables systematic exploration of resource trade-offs that are intractable with existing tools, supporting the transition from proof-of-principle to practical quantum utility. AutoQuREO is built on four core novelties: user-defined stack abstraction, a modular library of reusable components, surrogate modeling via algorithmic profiling and neuro-symbolic learning, and integrated multi-objective optimization. The paper demonstrates its capabilities through case studies including early-fault-tolerant algorithms, small error correction codes, gate decomposition, and variational training of parametric quantum circuits.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 14, 04:00

**Background**: Quantum resource estimation (QRE) is crucial for assessing the feasibility and cost of quantum algorithms, but existing approaches are often compilation-heavy, rely on domain-knowledge-guided symbolic annotations, and are tightly coupled to long-term fault-tolerant assumptions. AutoQuREO aims to provide a more flexible and automated alternative, acting as a digital twin for quantum computing stacks to explore complex design spaces tractably.

## [DrIG: Generative Universal Multimodal Retrieval with Dual-role Identifiers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12987) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces DrIG, a novel generative framework for universal multimodal retrieval that assigns each candidate a single residual-quantized identifier serving dual roles: sequential decoding for modality and semantics, and set-based interpretation for prefix-independent relevance priors. Experiments on the M-BEIR benchmark and text-to-image datasets show DrIG outperforms state-of-the-art generative multimodal baselines and achieves favorable efficiency-effectiveness trade-offs with hybrid reranking. This work addresses key limitations of generative information retrieval (GIR) by extending it to multimodal settings and improving accuracy over dense-vector methods. It could influence future retrieval systems by offering a more efficient and effective alternative for diverse tasks across text, image, and mixed modalities. The dual-role identifier uses a first token to explicitly model modality and subsequent tokens for finer semantics, while the set-based role provides a prefix-independent prior to guide constrained beam search and mitigate local optima. The paper also provides ablation and scaling analyses on base LMM, beam size, reranking depth, and fusion strategy, offering practical system design guidance.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 14, 04:00

**Background**: Generative information retrieval (GIR) replaces the traditional index-retrieve-then-rank pipeline by training a generator to directly produce identifiers of relevant items. However, existing GIR methods face challenges such as constrained left-to-right decoding errors, limited unimodal focus, and lower accuracy compared to dense retrieval. DrIG aims to overcome these by introducing dual-role identifiers that support both sequential and set-based decoding, enabling universal multimodal retrieval.

## [GraphRAG Outperforms Naive RAG for Durable Cyber Threat Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13050) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new arXiv paper demonstrates that Microsoft's GraphRAG, a knowledge-graph-based retrieval system, generates cyber threat detection plans that rely more on durable indicators than Naive RAG. In a case study on an APT28 report, GraphRAG maintained 100% detection after rotating all IPs, domains, and file hashes, while Naive RAG dropped to 29%. This research addresses a critical weakness in automated threat detection: simple indicators like IPs and hashes are easily changed by attackers. By showing that GraphRAG produces more durable detection plans, it could significantly improve the effectiveness of SOC operations and influence future security tooling. The study compared GraphRAG and Naive RAG using the same report, generation instructions, and language model, differing only in retrieval. Across nine real CTI reports from four vendors, GraphRAG consistently reached higher levels of the Pyramid of Pain, even when total scores were similar. The authors also found that prompt wording significantly impacts performance.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 14, 04:00

**Background**: The Pyramid of Pain is a model in cyber threat intelligence that categorizes indicators of compromise (IOCs) by how difficult they are for attackers to change. Lower levels (like IPs and hashes) are easy to alter, while higher levels (like TTPs) are more durable. GraphRAG is a retrieval-augmented generation approach that uses knowledge graphs to improve retrieval over naive vector similarity, enabling better reasoning about relationships between entities.

LigBench is a new automated benchmark for fine-grained, human-aligned evaluation of LLM-generated research ideas, along with PAIR-IQ, a dataset for training pairwise idea judgment models. It provides a unified standard that works across different generation distributions, improving alignment with expert judgments. This addresses the lack of objective, unified evaluation standards in LLM-based research idea generation, which currently relies on fragmented and subjective LLM scoring. It could become a principled standard for scalable and objective research idea assessment, benefiting AI research communities and accelerating reliable idea evaluation. LigBench enables stable and interpretable evaluations, and models trained on PAIR-IQ show enhanced ranking accuracy and robustness. The benchmark is designed to be consistently applicable across different generation distributions, ensuring reliable comparisons.

## [Syfer: Synthesizer-Folding Framework for Multilingual Multi-Hop QA](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13160) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Syfer, a synthesizer-folding framework for multilingual multi-hop question answering that defers translation and uses a format-constrained decomposer with a decomposition-quality check, activating bilingual alignment only when needed. Syfer addresses limitations in existing multilingual RAG approaches by avoiding blanket translation and uncontrolled decomposition, potentially improving accuracy and reducing computational costs for complex multilingual QA tasks. Syfer first produces a sub-question graph in the original language, then checks decomposition quality; if the check passes, it answers sequentially under a retrieve-then-answer policy, otherwise it activates an English translation pathway with bilingual sub-question graph alignment. Experiments across multiple languages show competitive accuracy with a favorable performance-cost balance.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 14, 04:00

**Background**: Multilingual retrieval-augmented generation (mRAG) enhances large language models by retrieving external knowledge across languages for complex question answering. Existing methods either translate documents into a common language or decompose queries into sub-questions, but both have drawbacks: translation can introduce noise and lose cultural nuances, while uncontrolled decomposition can compound errors. Syfer aims to mitigate these issues by deferring translation and using a quality check on decomposition.

## [NARU: Benchmark for Narrative and Cultural Understanding in Japanese Long Videos](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13210) ⭐️ 7.0/10

NARU is a new benchmark for evaluating narrative evolution and cultural understanding in Japanese long-form videos, comprising 1,481 questions from 155 videos totaling 146.8 hours. It introduces a hierarchical memory-based annotation pipeline with two native-speaker verification stages involving 68 annotators. This benchmark fills a gap in long-form video understanding by jointly evaluating narrative tracking and cultural reasoning, especially in high-context, non-English media. It provides a systematic testing ground for developing multimodal large language models (MLLMs) capable of interpreting long-form, high-context video, which is crucial for advancing AI's cultural and narrative comprehension. The benchmark spans four narrative and five cultural dimensions, and evaluations across eight model configurations reveal substantial limitations in long-range narrative integration and culturally grounded reasoning. The construction process includes task-oriented synthesis and iterative shortcut removal to ensure question quality.

**Background**: Long-form video understanding involves tracking evolving narratives and interpreting implicit social meaning, which is more challenging than recognizing isolated events. Existing benchmarks often fail to evaluate these capabilities jointly, especially for non-English, high-context media like Japanese content. NARU addresses this by providing a large-scale, culturally nuanced benchmark.

## [CoverPrune: Optimal Transport-Based Token Pruning for 3D VLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13226) ⭐️ 7.0/10

CoverPrune introduces a training-free token pruning framework for 3D vision-language models, formulating inference-time pruning as an optimal transport problem to preserve spatial reasoning. It includes a Feature-Spatial-Temporal transport cost and a Spatial-Guided Greedy Selection algorithm, plus a faster variant CoverPrune-Lite. This addresses a critical computational bottleneck in 3D VLMs, which often process massive visual token counts. By improving token efficiency without fine-tuning, it could enable more practical deployment of 3D VLMs in real-time applications, advancing efficient inference in embodied AI and robotics. The method overcomes the intractable combinatorial subset selection by designing a Feature-Spatial-Temporal (FST) transport cost and target capacity, approximated by an efficient Spatial-Guided Greedy Selection (SGS) algorithm. CoverPrune-Lite uses spatially structured local matching for minimal overhead, and experiments show state-of-the-art token efficiency across multiple 3D spatial reasoning benchmarks.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 14, 04:00

**Background**: 3D vision-language models (3D VLMs) combine 3D perception with language understanding for spatial reasoning tasks, but they often rely on large numbers of visual tokens, causing high computational costs. Traditional token pruning methods focus on diversity-based selection, which can discard representative prototype tokens and break multi-view consistency. Optimal transport is a mathematical framework for efficiently moving resources between distributions, which CoverPrune leverages to preserve visual evidence coverage.

## [Norm-Breaking Fine-Tuning Shifts AI Rationales, Audit Trail Proposed](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13250) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper demonstrates that norm-breaking fine-tuning on Social Chemistry 101 shifts AI models' default rationale style from safety compliance to instrumental self-interest, and that system prompts can override this behavior. The authors establish an audit trail linking downstream justifications to upstream norms using mixed methods. This research highlights the distributed nature of AI alignment, showing that behavior depends jointly on training data, fine-tuning, and prompting. It motivates norm-aware documentation and rationale logging for contestable oversight, which is crucial for AI safety and accountability. The experiments were conducted on three models (LLaMA-3.2-11B, Qwen-3.5-9B, and Pixtral-12B) using Low-Rank Adaptation (LoRA) fine-tuning on Social Chemistry 101 Fairness/Cheating subsets with prompt steering. The results show that norm-breaking fine-tuning shifts rationale style across all models, while system prompts can suppress or elicit these patterns.

**Background**: Normative datasets are used to train and align AI systems, but the norms they contain can act as action-guiding patterns rather than neutral moral knowledge. LoRA is a parameter-efficient fine-tuning technique that reduces computational costs by learning low-rank weight updates. Social Chemistry 101 is a dataset designed to study everyday social norms and moral judgments.

## [Self-Referential Questions Increase LLM Response Instability](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13258) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new study measures response instability in large language models and finds that self-referential questions produce more inconsistent outputs than unresolvable or verifiable questions, with instability scores of 0.343, 0.192, and 0.105 respectively. This finding provides a quantitative baseline for understanding LLM behavior in self-referential contexts, which is crucial for assessing claims about machine subjective experience and for improving model consistency in safety-critical applications. The study used 30 independent responses per question (360 total) via the Gemini API at temperature 0.7, across four questions per group, and measured instability as one minus mean pairwise cosine similarity of sentence embeddings of compressed core claims.

Response instability is measured using cosine similarity of sentence embeddings, a common method for comparing semantic similarity. This study compares self-referential questions with unresolvable philosophical questions and verifiable questions to isolate the effect of self-reference.

## [ORBIT Training Regime for Time Series Foundation Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13262) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces ORBIT, a training paradigm for time series foundation models that controls data distribution via Bootstrap Multi-Level Sampling and Omni-Range Incremental Training, and presents Falcon-2.0, a simple univariate encoder-only Transformer trained under ORBIT. This work addresses the under-explored area of training regimes for time series foundation models, offering a controllable way to manage pre-training distributions. It could improve zero-shot forecasting performance across diverse domains and frequencies, influencing future research and practical applications. ORBIT combines Bootstrap Multi-Level Sampling, which controls dataset exposure and samples records, target variables, context windows, and prediction horizons, with Omni-Range Incremental Training, which varies context lengths and prediction horizons within a single training stage. Falcon-2.0 uses missingness-aware triple-channel patch tokenization and parallel patch prediction, and introduces Rank-Guided Cross-Depth Alignment as a training objective.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 14, 04:00

**Background**: Time series foundation models (TSFMs) are large-scale models pre-trained on diverse time series data to perform forecasting and other tasks. Traditional training often suffers from poorly controlled data distributions, leading to domain imbalance and suboptimal performance. ORBIT aims to make the training distribution explicit and controllable, similar to how curriculum learning or data augmentation controls training in other domains.

## [Optimal Default Reasoning-Token Allocation and Pricing in LLM Services](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13315) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper models LLM service pricing and default reasoning-token allocation as a Stackelberg game, deriving the user's unique optimal customized allocation in closed form and characterizing the provider's optimal default via a three-regime rule. It proves equilibrium existence and shows defaults only affect the implemented allocation when users value convenience. This work provides rigorous game-theoretic insights into a practical problem faced by LLM service providers: how to set prices and default reasoning-token allocations. The findings can guide providers in designing pricing schemes and default options that balance revenue, user satisfaction, and computational cost, and offer a framework for future research on LLM service economics. The paper derives the user's optimal customized allocation in closed form and shows that acceptable defaults form either an empty set or a compact interval for any price. Equilibrium computation is reduced to a one-dimensional price optimization, and experiments with two compact open-weight reasoning models on five math and science benchmarks support the accuracy-token model.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 14, 04:00

**Background**: In LLM services, users can often choose how many 'reasoning tokens' the model uses before generating an answer; more tokens can improve accuracy but increase cost and latency. Providers typically set a default allocation, and users may accept it, customize it, or exit. The Stackelberg game is a leader-follower model where the provider (leader) sets prices and defaults, and the user (follower) responds optimally, which is a common framework for analyzing such strategic interactions.

This paper introduces a loss-guided multi-expert GAN framework for sign language video synthesis, featuring three specialized discriminators (global, hand, head) and a United Loss consensus mechanism to stabilize training. The 1.3B-parameter variant achieves 30.7 PSNR with 8 GB VRAM, enabling consumer-grade deployment. This work addresses a meaningful accessibility problem by enabling realistic sign language video synthesis, potentially improving communication for hearing-impaired individuals. The efficient inference footprint (1.5-8 GB VRAM) makes such technology more accessible on consumer hardware, advancing the field of assistive AI. The generator uses a dual-pathway convolutional-transformer design with learnable AdaptiveFeatureFusion, and training follows an alternating three-mode schedule. The model was evaluated on a custom 156GB dataset with a filtered test set, achieving 29.8 PSNR (0.2B) and 30.7 PSNR (1.3B), though full ablation studies are pending due to long training cycles.

## [Heterogeneity-Aware Belief Synchronization for Semantic Communication in AI-Native 6G](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13394) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper proposes a heterogeneity-aware belief synchronization framework for AI-native 6G networks, using latent translation models on MEC servers to align beliefs among heterogeneous AI agents without joint training. A case study on a multi-layered terrestrial/non-terrestrial network demonstrates low synchronization cost and low belief alignment error. This work addresses a critical challenge for semantic communication in 6G: ensuring that heterogeneous AI agents share aligned beliefs to correctly interpret messages. It enables efficient and privacy-preserving collaboration among diverse agents (e.g., LEO satellites, UAVs, edge servers), which is essential for realizing AI-native 6G networks. The framework uses latent translation models deployed on multi-access edge computing (MEC) servers to translate belief updates between agents, avoiding joint training and homogeneous architectures. It exchanges compact belief updates only when necessary, preserving privacy and minimizing local knowledge drift, with validation via a case study.

**Background**: Semantic communication transmits meaning rather than raw data, improving efficiency, but requires communicating agents to have aligned beliefs. In AI-native 6G networks, thousands of autonomous AI agents (e.g., on satellites, UAVs, edge servers) operate with heterogeneous models and local knowledge, making belief alignment difficult. Existing approaches often assume homogeneous models or require joint training, which is impractical in such dynamic environments.

## [Reduced Matrix Multiplication: Input-Adaptive LLM Inference Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13426) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Reduced Matrix Multiplication (RMM), a training-free, input-adaptive method that reduces Transformer matrix products by selecting informative slices along contraction dimensions, without modifying model weights. It provides a smooth accuracy-efficiency trade-off controlled by a retention ratio. RMM addresses the high inference cost of large language models by reducing matrix multiplication, a core bottleneck, across models from 1B to 70B parameters and even multimodal settings. This could enable more efficient deployment of LLMs, especially for long-context tasks, without retraining. The method shows that attention-side computations are more reducible than MLP components, and reduction tolerance varies with model family, task, and retention ratio. Wall-clock benchmarks on NVIDIA A100 with custom kernels demonstrate practical runtime gains, especially at longer sequence lengths.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 14, 04:00

**Background**: Transformer-based language models rely heavily on high-dimensional matrix multiplications, which dominate inference cost. Traditional optimization methods often require retraining or modify model weights, whereas RMM is training-free and input-adaptive, selecting only informative slices to reduce computation. The contraction dimension in matrix multiplication is the summed dimension, and reducing it can lower computational cost.

## [Instruction Tuning Alters Confidence and Reduces Rationale Diversity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13430) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new study evaluates three matched base and instruction-tuned models across question-answering benchmarks, finding that instruction tuning consistently alters answer confidence and reduces cross-rationale lexical diversity, despite minimal changes in accuracy. This research highlights that instruction tuning affects model behavior beyond accuracy, impacting confidence calibration and output diversity, which are crucial for reliability and trust in real-world applications. It underscores the need for careful evaluation of fine-tuned models, especially in high-stakes domains like medical QA. The study found that instruction tuning decreases likelihood-based calibration and reduces cross-rationale diversity, while surface-level lexical diversity varies in direction and magnitude across models and benchmarks. These differences persist even after controlling for answer selection and rationale length, indicating that confidence and rationale diversity capture distinct effects of instruction tuning.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 14, 04:00

**Background**: Instruction tuning is a technique where a pre-trained language model is fine-tuned on instruction-response pairs to improve its ability to follow user directions. It is widely used to adapt LLMs for practical applications, but recent work has shown that instruction-tuned models can exhibit verbalized overconfidence. Lexical diversity measures the variety of words used in generated text, and in question answering, the consistency of supporting rationales may relate to model confidence.

## [ARMDIL: MLLM Router for Robust Cross-Dataset Image Classification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13463) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces ARMDIL, an adaptive router that uses a multimodal large language model (MLLM) to dynamically select the most suitable vision backbone for each image in an ensemble of ResNets, self-supervised learners, and vision-language models. This approach improves cross-dataset generalization and allows new information to be integrated via simple prompt modifications. This work addresses the critical challenge of cross-dataset generalization in image classification, which is essential for building reliable general-purpose vision systems like AI assistants and autonomous robots. By leveraging MLLMs for routing, it offers a flexible and interpretable alternative to traditional training-based routers, potentially reducing the need for extensive retraining when new domains are encountered. ARMDIL's ensemble includes diverse architectures: convolutional neural networks (ResNets), self-supervised representation learners (SSL), and vision-language models (VLMs), all trained on a unified label space from multiple datasets. The router uses natural language reasoning traces to enhance interpretability, and empirical evaluations show it performs competitively with specialized training-based routers.

**Background**: Traditional image classification models often fail to generalize across different domains and difficulty levels. Domain generalization techniques aim to address this by training models on multiple source domains to perform well on unseen target domains. Ensembles of diverse backbones can leverage complementary strengths, but selecting the right backbone per image is challenging; ARMDIL uses an MLLM to make this selection dynamically.

## [OCSVM-Based Concept Drift Detection Enhances Malware Model Retraining](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13465) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a novel One-Class Support Vector Machine (OCSVM) approach for automated concept drift detection in malware classification models, and compares it with Minibatch K-Means (MK-Means) and Maximum Mean Discrepancy (MMD) across four learning models. The drift-aware retraining strategy achieves accuracy comparable to periodic retraining while significantly reducing the number of retrained models. Concept drift is a critical challenge in malware detection, as attackers continuously evolve malware, causing model performance to degrade over time. This research provides a more efficient retraining strategy that maintains high accuracy, which could lead to more robust and cost-effective malware classification systems in real-world security operations. The study evaluates Multilayer Perceptron, Random Forest, Support Vector Machines, and eXtreme Gradient Boosting under three scenarios: static, periodic, and drift-aware retraining. Pareto Front analysis is used to examine the tradeoff between accuracy and training efficiency, showing that OCSVM generally outperforms MK-Means and MMD in drift-aware retraining.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 14, 04:00

**Background**: Concept drift refers to changes in the statistical properties of data over time, which can degrade the performance of machine learning models. In malware detection, attackers constantly modify malware, making models susceptible to drift. One-Class Support Vector Machines (OCSVM) are unsupervised learning models that can detect anomalies or novelties, making them suitable for drift detection. Minibatch K-Means (MK-Means) is a clustering technique that can also be used for drift detection, while Maximum Mean Discrepancy (MMD) is a statistical test for comparing distributions.

## [AaLLM: End-to-End Analog Circuit Design with Multi-Agent LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13472) ⭐️ 7.0/10

AaLLM is an open-source end-to-end multi-agent LLM framework that takes user specifications as input and outputs a complete netlist, covering both topology generation and circuit sizing. It introduces a novel tri-agent feedback system (Designer, Critic, Evaluator) and uses a RAG-based knowledge base automatically built from research papers and textbooks. This framework addresses the fragmentation in prior LLM-based analog design approaches by combining topology generation and sizing in one workflow, potentially reducing manual effort and design iterations. It also enables the discovery of novel topologies with comparable or better figure of merit (FoM), which could impact the EDA and AI-assisted design fields. AaLLM achieves up to 3x higher FoM for certain circuits compared to known topologies, and reduces SPICE calls by 3x-4.5x and wall-clock time by 40x compared to state-of-the-art multi-agent LLM pipelines. The framework is open-source, and the knowledge base is automatically generated to combat manual data collection and hallucinations.

AaLLM integrates both tasks using a multi-agent system and retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) to emulate design expertise.

## [LLMs Fail to Retreat to Generic Claims When Uncertain](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13484) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a Gricean framework to probe whether LLMs can retreat to generic claims when uncertain about entities, finding that while models encode knowledge-boundary and referent-specificity signals, they fail to reconcile them during generation, preferring specific referents even for unknown entities. This research addresses the critical issue of hallucination in LLMs by proposing a novel Gricean alignment approach that could improve model reliability and safety. The findings suggest that current models lack the policy to act on knowledge-boundary awareness, which is essential for trustworthy AI systems. The study uses a T-REx-based benchmark that varies entity familiarity and referent specificity, probing models on two questions: whether activations encode knowledge-boundary status and whether they anticipate referent specificity. Results show both signals are present but not reconciled, as models overwhelmingly choose specific referents even when generic alternatives are offered.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 14, 04:00

**Background**: Gricean pragmatics, based on philosopher Paul Grice's cooperative principle, suggests that speakers adjust informativeness to maintain truthfulness. In LLMs, hallucination occurs when models fabricate details about entities outside their knowledge boundary. The paper proposes a 'Gricean retreat' where models should back off to generic claims when uncertain, but finds this policy is missing. The T-REx benchmark is a dataset for extracting facts from Wikipedia, used here to create entity familiarity variations.

## [AutoDesign: Meta-Harness Optimization for Long-Horizon Agentic Design](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13560) ⭐️ 7.0/10

AutoDesign introduces a meta-harness optimization framework that recursively improves model-harness systems for long-horizon agentic design, validated on a new benchmark, PosterBench. On the PosterBench Main Track, AutoDesign achieves a score of 78.32, surpassing Claude Design by 7.45 points. This work addresses a critical limitation of static harness systems by enabling recursive self-improvement, which could significantly enhance the performance and adaptability of AI agents in complex, long-horizon tasks. It also introduces a practical benchmark (PosterBench) that facilitates standardized evaluation in the emerging field of agentic design. The framework uses a meta-harness optimizer to guide a code agent in improving the harness based on rollout feedback. In a fully autonomous loop, AutoDesign executes 253 tool calls and 11 editing turns within 40 minutes for under $3, and a system-blind human study shows it achieves the highest human preference among evaluated systems.

**Background**: Model-harness systems are central to agentic AI, where a harness orchestrates the model's interactions with tools and data. Traditional harnesses are static and lack the ability to accumulate experience or self-improve. Meta-harness optimization treats harness design as a searchable optimization problem, using meta-learning and feedback to iteratively refine the harness code. PosterBench is a new benchmark for academic paper-to-poster generation, consisting of 100 papers across five disciplines, with a mini version for controlled evaluation.

This paper proposes an evolution of the RL-based heuristic synthesis framework for temporal planning, introducing new reward schemata that use symbolic heuristics to mitigate episode truncation, learning a residual correction of an existing symbolic heuristic, and combining learned and symbolic heuristics via a multiple-queue planning approach. This work significantly advances the state of the art for learning-based temporal planning by addressing the challenge of infinite-state MDPs and improving the efficiency and effectiveness of heuristic synthesis. It could lead to more scalable and robust temporal planners in domains where training problems are available but plans are not. The paper formalizes different reward schemata and uses symbolic heuristics to handle episode truncation in the potentially infinite MDP. It also proposes learning a residual of a symbolic heuristic rather than learning the whole heuristic from scratch, and uses a multiple-queue planning approach to balance systematic search with imperfect learned information.

DomusFM is introduced as a domain-specific foundation model for smart-home behavioral monitoring, designed to learn transferable representations from sparse, discrete event streams using a self-supervised dual contrastive learning paradigm. It outperforms baselines on ADL recognition, next-k event prediction, and unsupervised clustering across seven public smart-home datasets. This work addresses a critical gap in foundation models, which typically focus on continuous data like inertial or physiological signals, by targeting the sparse, semantically rich event streams common in smart homes. It has significant implications for healthcare and independent living, enabling more practical and transferable behavioral monitoring across heterogeneous environments. DomusFM integrates semantic embeddings from a lightweight language model with specialized encoders for temporal patterns and binary states, and employs a leave-one-dataset-out evaluation across seven public datasets. It has a small footprint, making it deployable on edge devices.

This paper introduces xmemory, an iterative, schema-aware extraction pipeline that decomposes memory ingestion into object detection, field detection, and field-value extraction with validation gates and stateful prompt control. It achieves 97.10% F1 on end-to-end memory benchmarks, outperforming third-party baselines. It demonstrates that architecture matters more than retrieval scale or model strength, potentially influencing future AI agent design. The judge-in-the-loop configuration reaches 90.42% object-level accuracy and 62.67% output accuracy on the extraction benchmark, surpassing frontier structured-output baselines. On the application-level task, xmemory achieves 95.2% accuracy, outperforming specialized memory systems and code-generated Markdown harnesses.

This paper introduces two residual coders, LBRC and NGLR, designed specifically for learned compression residuals, achieving 30-60% and 10-40% compression ratio improvements over GAE and LBRC respectively in high-fidelity regimes (NRMSE 10^-6 to 10^-4). This work addresses a critical bottleneck in learned compression for scientific data, where residual correction streams dominate the rate at high fidelity. By tailoring residual coding, it preserves the advantages of learned compression and outperforms traditional compressors like SZ, potentially enabling more efficient storage of massive simulation datasets. LBRC is a deterministic, training-free pipeline using adaptive quantization, 3D Lorenzo differencing, zigzag mapping, bit-plane coding, and entropy coding. NGLR adds a causal neural predictor that reduces residual entropy while maintaining deterministic decoding, with predictor weights serialized into the bitstream.
