## [ReTeX: Recovering Task Experts from Multi-Task Merged Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.26902) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces ReTeX, a framework that predicts and corrects parameter interference in multi-task merged models, recovering task-specific experts from a single checkpoint. It also proposes a router-free task identifier based on SVD subspace signatures, achieving over 95% of individual-expert performance in vision and NLP tasks. This work addresses a critical challenge in multi-task model merging—parameter interference—by enabling efficient recovery of task experts without storing redundant components. It offers a fresh perspective on interference as affine transformations and introduces a router-free identifier, which could significantly improve the practicality and efficiency of multi-task models in real-world applications. ReTeX models parameter interference as affine transformations approximated by additive offsets, and predicts these offsets to undo interference. The router-free task identifier uses SVD subspace signatures computed offline, selecting the task with the smallest projection residual for a given input, and the framework also shows emergent adaptive interpolation for out-of-distribution tasks.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 14, 04:00

**Background**: Multi-task model merging consolidates several task-specific models into a single unified model, often using simple parameter arithmetic. However, static merging suffers from parameter interference, where combining parameters degrades performance on individual tasks. Dynamic merging methods attempt to address this but often require storing and loading redundant expert components at inference, which is costly. ReTeX aims to recover task experts from a single merged checkpoint without such overhead.

It achieves 90-100% feasibility on elementwise and normalization operators, 56% on fusion operators, and up to 6.65x speedup on 1B Pangu model inference kernels on Huawei Ascend 910B. The framework incorporates a knowledge-orchestrated generation system to overcome platform knowledge deficits and a stage-adaptive agentic evolution strategy to balance exploration and convergence. Experiments on six operators across five pattern categories show knowledge injection monotonically improves feasibility from 57% to 86% on elementwise operators, indicating general benefit.

<a id="item-257"></a>
## [DAPD: Dual-Anchored Policy Distillation to Fix Privilege Illusion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.01735) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Dual-Anchored Policy Distillation (DAPD), a framework with two levels of anchoring (Dual-Path and Dual-Source) to resolve information asymmetry in on-policy self-distillation. Experiments show DAPD outperforms OPSD on Qwen3-4B by +2.00 points on average, with gains persisting across scales (+2.69 at 4B and +2.78 at 32B). This addresses a critical failure mode in on-policy self-distillation for language models, which is widely used for post-training. By mitigating the privilege illusion, DAPD can improve the reliability and performance of distilled models, benefiting the AI community and downstream applications. DAPD introduces Dual-Path Anchoring (DPA) with a self-conditioned bridge to align reference and rollout behavior along matched-information paths, and Dual-Source Anchoring (DSA) to apply these paths in both directions, reducing reliance on privileged reference guidance while preserving correctness supervision. The paper is a preprint on arXiv (2608.01735) and has not yet undergone broad community validation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 14, 04:00

**Background**: On-policy self-distillation (OPSD) trains a single model as both student and teacher, where the teacher sees privileged information (e.g., ground-truth solutions) and the student only sees the problem. This can cause a 'privilege illusion' where the student learns behaviors dependent on privileged information that is unavailable at inference, degrading performance. DAPD aims to fix this by anchoring the student's behavior to non-privileged information paths.

## [Surrogate Substitution Preserves PHI Detectability in Clinical Text](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03172) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new multi-detector equivalence study demonstrates that structure-preserving surrogate substitution in de-identified clinical text maintains PHI detectability on masked spans, with recall moving from 76.1% to 74.9% across 11 detectors, 7 benchmarks, and 7 languages. The study introduces a paired evaluation protocol using TOST equivalence testing and a surrogate-failure typology. This work provides rigorous evidence that surrogate substitution does not degrade downstream PHI detection, which is crucial for balancing data utility and privacy in clinical NLP. The open-source protocol enables any structure-preserving transform to be audited, potentially improving de-identification practices across the industry. The study uses TOST equivalence testing with a +/-2-point margin, showing statistical equivalence (p ~ 3e-9) and preserved detector ranking. Residual loss is concentrated in malformed and out-of-distribution surrogates (e.g., truncation 'Chicago' -> 'Illino'), not in well-formed substitutions. The authors release evaluation subsets, scoring code, and an interactive dashboard at https://custodianai.pages.dev.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 14, 04:00

**Background**: De-identification in clinical NLP often replaces protected health information (PHI) with placeholders like [NAME], but structure-preserving de-identification substitutes realistic same-type surrogates (e.g., 'Anna S.' becomes 'Maria S.') to maintain text fluency and downstream tool compatibility. Equivalence testing, specifically the two one-sided t-tests (TOST) procedure, is used to determine whether two treatments are statistically equivalent within a pre-defined margin, which is more appropriate than null-hypothesis significance testing for large sample sizes.

## [EU-AI-Act-Compliant Load Forecasting Beats ENTSO-E in Live Challenge](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.05018) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A 41-day live challenge evaluated an STLF pipeline for the German transmission grid, built with the spotforecast2-safe library, and it outperformed the official ENTSO-E day-ahead forecast. The pipeline also demonstrated that small, transparent local models (macl2l) are competitive with large foundation models like chronos-2. This work addresses the critical intersection of AI safety, regulatory compliance (EU AI Act), and energy forecasting, providing practical evidence that safety-critical AI can be both compliant and high-performing. It sets a precedent for developing AI systems in regulated industries, potentially influencing how future AI models are deployed in critical infrastructure. The pipeline uses ENTSO-E data, includes gap- and anomaly-aware preprocessing, calendar and weather covariates, and a forecasting algorithm. The challenge implementation, submission history, and leaderboard are publicly available, and the spotforecast2-safe library is open-source on PyPI.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 14, 04:00

**Background**: Short-term load forecasting (STLF) is crucial for electric power grid operations, but traditional accuracy-focused approaches are insufficient for safety-critical environments. The EU AI Act imposes strict requirements on AI systems used in critical infrastructure, including transparency, determinism, and fail-safe handling. The spotforecast2-safe library is designed to meet these requirements by implementing a safety-first architecture, and this challenge tested its practical viability against a real-world baseline.

## [NDS: FAIR Data Infrastructure for AI-Driven Nutrition Research](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10363) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Nutrition Data Service (NDS), a source-preserving infrastructure that operationalizes FAIR data principles for AI agents, enabling description resolution, typed crosswalks, and machine-readable interfaces. NDS outperforms existing benchmarks on NutriBench and ensures reproducible analyses. This work addresses critical data ambiguity and reproducibility challenges in AI-mediated nutrition research, potentially setting a new standard for data infrastructure in scientific domains. It demonstrates that explicit data identity and crosswalk policies are essential for reliable agent-driven analyses. NDS uses typed crosswalks to connect independently released resources, and its machine-readable interfaces support replayable and auditable analyses. In a person-level glycemic-index analysis, pinned NDS inputs produced identical outputs across models and repeated runs, while open-web reconstruction remained unstable.

The paper introduces MBA-Bench, the first multimodal benchmark for training and evaluating LLM-based business ideation agents, containing 30K samples across six domains. It also presents two agents, MBA-b and MBA-k, trained with novel reward objectives, which outperform baselines by up to 77.1%. This benchmark addresses a critical gap in business ideation evaluation, which has been text-only despite the multimodal nature of real-world contexts. It provides a standardized way to assess and improve AI agents for business innovation, potentially accelerating the adoption of multimodal AI in business applications. The benchmark uses GPT-4o to generate reference ideas via retrieval query generation, market evidence retrieval, and evidence-augmented synthesis, and evaluates agents with MLLM-as-a-Judge across six business-oriented criteria. MBA-b and MBA-k are trained with LoRA-based supervised fine-tuning and group relative policy optimization, with MBA-k optimizing eight criteria including creativity and feasibility.

## [LLM-Generated ICU Summaries Improve Mortality Prediction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2411.16818) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This study introduces a multi-representational framework that fuses LLM-generated expert summaries of ICU notes with physiological data, achieving AUPRC 0.4977 and AUROC 0.8429 on MIMIC-III, significantly outperforming physiology alone (AUPRC 0.3625, AUROC 0.7770). This work demonstrates that LLM-generated summaries can enhance clinical prediction models, offering a scalable way to leverage unstructured notes. It also provides a rigorous redundancy analysis, clarifying the added value of summaries beyond the original notes, which is crucial for practical deployment. The study used MIMIC-III with 19,211 first ICU stays and 12.83% mortality, and evaluated on 3,843 held-out stays. Redundancy analysis via ridge recoverability showed that note embeddings explained 40.8% of summary-embedding variance, and a note-orthogonal residual retained only 28% of the gain (+0.0258 AUPRC).

**Background**: MIMIC-III is a large, freely-available database of deidentified health data from over 40,000 ICU patients at Beth Israel Deaconess Medical Center (2001-2012). AUPRC and AUROC are common metrics for evaluating classification models, especially under class imbalance. Ridge regression is a regularized linear regression technique that helps prevent overfitting.

## [Cueless EEG Imagined Speech Dataset and Benchmarks for Subject Identification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.09700) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a novel cueless EEG-based imagined speech paradigm, where subjects imagine pronouncing semantically meaningful words without any external cues, and presents a dataset of over 4,350 trials from 11 subjects across five sessions. The study benchmarks various classification methods, achieving a high accuracy of 97.93% for subject identification. This work addresses limitations of prior imagined speech studies that relied on visual or auditory cues, making the paradigm more natural and practical for real-world biometric applications. The high accuracy and novel approach could advance EEG-based biometrics and brain-computer interfaces, potentially leading to more secure and reliable identification systems. The study evaluates traditional machine learning methods (SVM, XGBoost), time-series foundation models, and EEG-specific deep learning architectures like EEG Conformer and Shallow ConvNet. A session-based hold-out validation strategy was used to prevent data leakage, ensuring reliable evaluation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 14, 04:00

Imagined speech involves mentally pronouncing words without vocalization, which can be used as a cognitive task in brain-computer interfaces. Previous studies often provided visual or auditory cues to guide subjects, but this work introduces a cueless approach where subjects select and imagine words from a predefined list naturally.

## [WaveFT: Wavelet-Domain Sparse Updates for Efficient Vision Fine-Tuning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.12532) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Wavelet Fine-Tuning (WaveFT), a novel PEFT method that learns sparse updates in the wavelet domain of weight matrices, enabling finer-grained control than LoRA's low-rank constraint. WaveFT has been officially integrated into the Hugging Face PEFT library. WaveFT addresses key limitations of existing PEFT methods like LoRA, offering higher representational capacity and better gradient coverage for vision tasks. Its inclusion in the Hugging Face PEFT library signals practical adoption, potentially benefiting researchers and practitioners seeking efficient fine-tuning alternatives. WaveFT leverages wavelet bases' semi-local receptive fields to aggregate spatially coherent gradients, avoiding destructive interference seen in global Fourier bases. Theoretical analysis shows sparse methods achieve high-rank updates, and experiments demonstrate state-of-the-art results among PEFT methods for vision tasks while performing comparably on NLP tasks.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 14, 04:00

**Background**: Parameter-Efficient Fine-Tuning (PEFT) methods like LoRA adapt large pretrained models by updating a small subset of parameters, reducing computational costs. LoRA uses low-rank matrices, but its discrete rank constraint limits fine-grained control. WaveFT instead applies sparse updates in the wavelet domain, which offers semi-local receptive fields that balance coverage and interference, potentially improving adaptation quality.

## [REHEARSE: Training-Free Method Improves LLM Confidence Calibration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.14390) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces REHEARSE, a training-free method that improves LLM verbal confidence calibration by having models learn from their own scored confidence experiences in a credence-calibration game. Across four LLMs, three benchmarks, and five random seeds, REHEARSE achieves the lowest average ECE among training-free methods, reducing average ECE by 58% relative to the uncalibrated baseline. This is significant because poor confidence calibration in LLMs limits their reliability in safety-critical applications. REHEARSE offers a training-free, post-hoc solution that can be applied to existing models without retraining, potentially improving trustworthiness across the AI ecosystem. REHEARSE uses a strictly proper scoring rule in a credence-calibration game to generate feedback, which is summarized into a post-game trajectory prefix capturing systematic over- or under-confidence. At inference, this prefix is applied to the chain-of-thought reasoning trace for each new question, enabling cross-instance calibration.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 14, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models often express confidence that is misaligned with actual correctness, a problem known as miscalibration. Existing prompt-based methods treat calibration as a one-shot inference problem, relying on instance-level reasoning or post-hoc self-assessment. The credence calibration game, originally developed for humans, uses proper scoring rules to encourage accurate confidence reporting. REHEARSE adapts this game to LLMs, allowing them to learn from their own confidence experiences without additional training.

## [CSICL: Gradual Code-Switching Boosts Multilingual LLM Performance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.05678) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces CSICL (Code-Switching In-Context Learning), an inference-time prompting strategy that gradually transitions from a target language to English within demonstrations and instructions. Across 4 LLMs, 6 datasets, and 10 languages, CSICL consistently outperforms cross-lingual in-context learning baselines, with average gains of 6.0pp in target languages and 4.8pp in unseen languages. This work addresses the persistent issue of English-centric latent representations in multilingual LLMs, which leads to uneven performance across languages. By providing a simple, training-free method to align representations at inference time, CSICL could improve multilingual capabilities, especially for low-resource languages, without additional fine-tuning, making it a practical contribution to more equitable multilingual systems. The method involves few-shot demonstrations that gradually code-switch from the target language to English, with an instruction explicitly asking the model to 'gradually translate' the query into English, think in English, and then answer in the target language. Gains are more pronounced in low-resource settings, with improvements of 14.7pp in target and 5.3pp in unseen languages, and the approach generalizes across language families.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 14, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models (LLMs) often rely on English-centric latent representations, even when processing other languages, leading to cross-lingual misalignment where semantically equivalent inputs produce divergent internal representations. Traditional approaches to cross-lingual transfer often use abrupt translation pivots, which can be inefficient or lossy. CSICL instead scaffolds the reasoning trajectory by gradually transitioning from the target language to English, aligning non-English inputs with the English-centric reasoning space. This is an inference-time method, meaning it requires no additional training or fine-tuning, making it easy to apply to existing models.

A new paper evaluates ten Unicode text watermarking methods across six large language models, including GPT-5, GPT-4o, and Llama 3.3, finding that the latest reasoning models can detect watermarked text. However, none of the models could extract the watermark without access to the source code. This study is significant because it highlights vulnerabilities in existing Unicode text watermarking techniques, which are used to protect digital text from unauthorized use and to distinguish AI-generated content. The findings can guide researchers and practitioners in choosing more secure watermarking methods and inform future research to address these security concerns. The study implemented ten Unicode text watermarking methods in a controlled testbed of three experiments, testing them against six LLMs: GPT-5, GPT-4o, Teuken 7B, Llama 3.3, Claude Sonnet 4, and Gemini 2.5 Pro. The results show that while detection is possible, extraction remains difficult without implementation details, suggesting that watermarking methods may still offer some protection against watermark removal.

## [LAMaS: Latency-Aware Orchestration for Multi-Agent Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.10560) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces LAMaS, a latency-aware orchestration framework for learning-based multi-agent systems, which optimizes critical path latency through constrained optimization with critical-path-aware credit assignment at training time and a lightweight runtime controller that prunes redundant agent interactions during inference. Experiments on four benchmarks show LAMaS reduces end-to-end latency by over 50% while maintaining competitive or better accuracy. This matters because latency is a critical bottleneck in multi-agent LLM systems, and existing orchestration methods largely ignore it, focusing only on cost and accuracy. LAMaS provides a systematic approach to latency optimization that can significantly improve user experience and enable real-time applications, potentially influencing future orchestration designs in the LLM agent ecosystem. LAMaS operates at two levels: training-time learning of latency-aware execution graphs via constrained optimization with critical-path-aware credit assignment, and inference-time adaptive pruning of redundant future agent interactions using a lightweight controller. The framework is modular and transfers to other MAS with minimal changes, consistently yielding latency reductions across four benchmarks.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 14, 04:00

**Background**: Multi-agent systems (MAS) coordinate multiple LLM-powered agents through structured workflows, which enhances reasoning but introduces high inference latency due to multi-step execution and repeated model invocations. End-to-end latency in such systems is governed by the critical execution path, so reducing total cost alone does not reliably reduce latency. Naive latency optimization can misassign operator-level credit and degrade task accuracy, making it a non-trivial challenge.

## [New Framework for Individuating Neural Architectures via Represented Processes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.11618) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper proposes a novel theoretical framework for individuating neural architectures based on the represented process at a receiver, using factorization and kernel-based distinctions, rather than just module syntax or computation graphs. It demonstrates that local and attention schemas can be distinction-equivalent after one injective prefix and inequivalent after another, revealing context dependence. This work could change how neural architectures are analyzed and compared, moving beyond surface-level syntax to the represented processes. It may impact architecture search, model interpretation, and the theoretical foundations of deep learning, though its immediate practical impact is unclear. The paper introduces the concept of an 'extensional shadow' that classifies unmarked surjective factorizations up to unique carrier re-presentation, but does not determine marked receiver organization. An exact two-token construction shows that local and attention schemas are distinction-equivalent after one injective prefix and inequivalent after another, even though neither prefix loses predecessor information.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 14, 04:00

**Background**: Neural architecture is often identified by module syntax, computation graphs, or the composite functions they realize. This paper studies the represented process available at a receiver, focusing on factorization and kernel-based distinctions to individuate architectures. The work is highly technical and relevant to AI/ML research, particularly in representation theory and architecture comparison.

## [MOSAIC: New Benchmark Evaluates LLMs' Moral, Social, Individual Dimensions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.00048) ⭐️ 7.0/10

MOSAIC is a new large-scale benchmark that jointly assesses the moral, social, and individual characteristics of large language models (LLMs). It comprises nine validated questionnaires and four platform-based games, totaling over 600 curated questions and scenarios, and is released as an extensible resource with a Python library. This benchmark addresses a critical gap in LLM evaluation by moving beyond the dominant Moral Foundation Theory (MFT) to include social values, personality traits, and individual characteristics. It provides the first empirical evidence that MFT alone is insufficient for comprehensive ethical evaluation, which is vital as LLMs are deployed in sensitive applications like healthcare and psychological support. MOSAIC includes nine validated questionnaires from moral philosophy, psychology, and social theory, plus four games probing morally ambiguous scenarios. The benchmark was validated across three models from different families, demonstrating its utility across all dimensions and highlighting the insufficiency of MFT.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 14, 04:00

**Background**: Moral Foundation Theory (MFT) is a social psychological framework proposing that human morality is based on innate foundations such as care, fairness, loyalty, authority, and purity. Existing LLM benchmarks rely heavily on MFT, but this overlooks other dimensions like social values and personality that shape ethical reasoning. MOSAIC aims to fill this gap by providing a more holistic evaluation tool.

## [CangjieBench: New Benchmark for Low-Resource Language Code Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.14501) ⭐️ 7.0/10

CangjieBench is a new contamination-free benchmark for the low-resource general-purpose programming language Cangjie, comprising 248 high-quality samples manually translated from HumanEval and ClassEval. It evaluates LLMs under four settings: Direct Generation, Syntax-Constrained Generation, Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG), and Agent, revealing that Syntax-Constrained Generation offers the best accuracy-cost trade-off. This benchmark addresses an underexplored area in LLM evaluation—low-resource general-purpose programming languages—and provides insights into how models generalize to unseen languages. The findings could guide developers and researchers in choosing effective strategies for code generation in such languages, potentially improving LLM support for emerging languages like Cangjie. The benchmark includes both Text-to-Code and Code-to-Code tasks, and the study observes that Code-to-Code translation often underperforms Text-to-Code generation, suggesting negative transfer. The Agent setting achieves state-of-the-art accuracy but incurs high token consumption, while Direct Generation performs poorly.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 14, 04:00

**Background**: Cangjie is a high-level, statically typed, general-purpose programming language developed by Huawei, first released in June 2024 for HarmonyOS NEXT app development. Low-resource programming languages lack sufficient training data for LLMs, making code generation challenging. Existing research on low-resource languages has focused on domain-specific languages (DSLs), leaving general-purpose languages underexplored. Contamination-free benchmarks, like LiveCodeBench, are designed to avoid overlap with training data, ensuring fair evaluation.

Doctorina MedBench introduces a dialogue-based evaluation framework for agent-based medical AI, simulating physician-patient interactions to assess diagnosis, treatment, and safety-critical handling. The framework was tested on a cohort of 254 physician-authored synthetic clinical cases. This benchmark addresses limitations of traditional QA-based medical benchmarks by evaluating multi-step clinical reasoning in interactive settings. It provides a complementary assessment tool for developing and monitoring agent-based medical AI systems, which is timely given the rapid adoption of such systems. The framework evaluates performance across task-level domains including diagnosis, differential diagnosis, treatment, safety-critical condition handling, and dialogue-step behavior, with the D.O.T.S. framework as a supplementary summary. It supports safety-oriented cases, category-based sampling, and regression-style comparisons across system versions.

## [VIBES: Bayesian-Guided VLM for Efficient Far-Field Anomaly Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.23724) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces VIBES, an asynchronous framework that uses Bayesian inference to guide Vision-Language Models (VLMs) to focus on localized regions for far-field anomaly detection in expressway surveillance videos. This approach improves detection accuracy and semantic interpretation while achieving real-time processing efficiency. This work addresses a practical challenge in traffic safety by enabling efficient and accurate detection of subtle abnormal vehicle motions in far-field surveillance, which is often missed by traditional methods. It also demonstrates a novel integration of Bayesian inference with VLM reasoning, potentially influencing future video analytics systems. VIBES operates on a 'zoom-in and reason-out' pipeline: an online kinematics-guided Bayesian module estimates a context-dependent normal-motion distribution and triggers the VLM only on selected frames and localized regions. This reduces computational overhead by avoiding continuous full-frame processing.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 14, 04:00

**Background**: Expressway surveillance is crucial for traffic management, but detecting anomalies in far-field vehicles is challenging due to their small size and subtle motions. Vision-Language Models (VLMs) offer strong semantic reasoning but are computationally heavy and can be distracted by irrelevant content when processing full frames. Bayesian inference provides a probabilistic framework to model normal motion patterns and identify deviations as potential anomalies.

## [SAFE-SVD: Sensitivity-Aware Compression for Physics Foundation Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.17985) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces SAFE-SVD, a novel compression framework that explicitly models loss-aware layer sensitivity in the output function space to compress physics foundation models (PFMs) while preserving physical fidelity. Experiments show substantial gains over existing methods, achieving significantly higher compression ratios while maintaining accuracy, in some cases by orders of magnitude. This work addresses the under-explored problem of compressing physics foundation models, which is crucial for deploying AI for Science applications in resource-constrained environments. By preserving physical fidelity, SAFE-SVD could enable more efficient and sustainable scientific foundation models, potentially opening a new subfield in AI for Science. The method explicitly models loss-aware layer sensitivity in the output function space, which is a key innovation compared to conventional compression methods that ignore the functional nature of physics data. The approach is validated across multiple models and datasets, demonstrating substantial gains in compression ratios while maintaining accuracy.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 14, 04:00

**Background**: Physics foundation models (PFMs) are a new trend in AI for Science, aiming to provide a 'train once, deploy anywhere' paradigm for scientific simulations. Model compression is essential for reducing memory use and accelerating inference, but it is under-explored for PFMs because physics data has a functional nature where partial derivatives encode spatiotemporal dynamics and exhibit high sensitivity to compression. Conventional compression methods often cause severe performance degradation when applied to PFMs.

## [Dimensional Balance Improves Large-Scale Spatiotemporal Prediction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.18793) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper proposes a scalable framework that uses entropy-based diagnostics to harmonize spatial and temporal feature representations, improving large-scale spatiotemporal prediction performance. The framework compresses spatial dimensionality via low-rank matrix embedding and extends the temporal horizon to capture long-range dependencies. This work addresses a significant bottleneck in spatiotemporal prediction, where existing methods often yield only incremental gains and limited cross-domain transferability. By introducing a novel diagnostic approach and a scalable framework, it offers a fresh perspective that could benefit fields like urban traffic, meteorology, and public health monitoring. The framework uses spatial and temporal entropy measures as diagnostic indicators of complexity mismatch, rather than guarantees of improved forecasting. Experiments on urban traffic, meteorological, and epidemic datasets show substantial accuracy gains, and the code is available on GitHub.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 14, 04:00

## [Stability-Aware Framework for Robust MLLM Checkpoint Selection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.18852) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper proposes a progressive framework for late-stage checkpoint selection in multimodal large language models (MLLMs), combining pointwise filtering, listwise ranking, and pairwise refinement with stability-aware ranking and evaluation subsampling. Experiments show that quality-aware curation of OCR-heavy inputs reduces ranking flip rate from 32.5% to 11.2% and increases inter-run agreement from 0.61 to 0.84. This work addresses a practical and under-explored problem in MLLM development: selecting the best checkpoint when evaluation signals are noisy and candidates are closely matched. The proposed framework provides a systematic way to quantify evaluation uncertainty, which can improve model deployment decisions and reduce wasted resources in training pipelines. The framework uses repeated evaluation-set subsampling to characterize ranking stability and percentile-based aggregation to account for lower- and upper-tail behavior. The paper also observes divergence between validation-loss progression and downstream checkpoint preference in two independent MLLM settings, and a public Qwen2.5-VL-7B reproduction across 11 checkpoints shows tightly clustered pointwise scores and tie-dominated final pairwise comparisons.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 14, 04:00

**Background**: Checkpoint selection is a critical step in training large language models, where practitioners often choose the final model based on validation loss or downstream evaluation metrics. However, when late-stage checkpoints are closely matched, small differences may be overshadowed by noise from finite evaluation samples, LLM-based judges, and ambiguous multimodal evidence. Stability-aware ranking and evaluation subsampling are techniques used to assess the reliability of model rankings under such uncertainty.

## [INSHAPE: Instance-Level Shapelets for Interpretable Time-Series Classification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.20088) ⭐️ 7.0/10

INSHAPE is a new framework that discovers instance-specific, non-overlapping temporal patterns (shapelets) for each time series, modeling their temporal dependencies to improve interpretability and classification performance. It also aggregates these instance-level shapelets into prototypical population-level shapelets, bridging local and global interpretability. This work addresses fundamental limitations of existing shapelet-based time-series classification, which often misalign with instance-specific features and ignore temporal dependencies. By providing instance-level interpretations, INSHAPE could lead to more accurate and trustworthy models in domains like healthcare and finance, where understanding individual predictions is crucial. INSHAPE was evaluated on 128 UCR and 30 UEA benchmark datasets, consistently outperforming state-of-the-art shapelet-based methods. The framework identifies variable-length, non-overlapping segments and models their temporal dependencies, offering clear instance-level interpretations while maintaining strong predictive performance.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 14, 04:00

**Background**: Time-series classification (TSC) is a common task in machine learning, and shapelets are discriminative subsequences that help make models more interpretable. Traditional shapelet methods focus on population-level patterns, which may not capture instance-specific nuances. INSHAPE aims to overcome this by discovering shapelets tailored to each time series, while also considering temporal interactions between patterns.

## [Annealed Softmax Greedy Achieves Near-Optimal Bayes Regret in Many-Armed Bandits](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.31034) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper proves that annealed softmax greedy achieves near-optimal Bayes regret in many-armed Bayesian Bernoulli bandits, specifically O~(m + T/m) and O~(√T) when m = Θ(√T). This provides a theoretical justification for uncertainty-agnostic policy updates like GRPO. This result offers a novel explanation for why uncertainty-agnostic updates in RLVR and GRPO can be effective, bridging bandit theory and modern LLM fine-tuning. It could influence future algorithm design and theoretical understanding of RLHF methods. The analysis relies on a β-regularity condition (specifically β=1) on the prior, which implies an abundance of near-optimal arms. The paper also shows that with a small number of arms, the same softmax policy can suffer linear regret, highlighting the importance of the many-armed regime.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 14, 04:00

**Background**: Multi-armed bandits are a classic framework for sequential decision-making under uncertainty, where an agent balances exploration and exploitation. GRPO is a reinforcement learning algorithm used to fine-tune large language models by sampling multiple completions per prompt and rewarding those that beat the group average, without explicitly modeling uncertainty.

## [Generative Inpainting for Hand Detection Under Distribution Shift](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.01896) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper evaluates whether generative inpainting of hand regions to add gloves and accessories can improve hand detection performance on real-world data. Across six experiments with YOLOv8n, multi-stage training schedules showed directional improvements in mAP@0.5 and narrowed the out-of-distribution gap for gloved hands. This work addresses a practical distribution shift in safety-critical hand detection, where public datasets underrepresent gloved or accessorized hands. The findings suggest that generative data augmentation can be beneficial, but its utility depends heavily on the training procedure, offering guidance for deploying such methods in real-world applications. The study uses a paired dataset of real images and synthetic counterparts, evaluating YOLOv8n detectors across six experiments (A-F), with four involving training under three random seeds. They report mAP@0.5 and mAP@0.5:0.95, and a two-stage experiment (train on real+synthetic, then fine-tune on real-only) directionally improved mAP@0.5, while a three-stage experiment preserved box-tightness best.

## [Certifiable Semantic Agreement Among LLM Agents: Admissibility Instrument Decides](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.07316) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces H-CSC, a protocol for LLM agents to achieve certifiable semantic agreement, and demonstrates that the admissibility instrument, not the protocol, determines coverage separation. Empirical results show a lexical predicate outperforms a fine-tuned encoder on adversarial attacks, and a similarity-preserving sketch detects substituted statements. This work addresses a critical challenge in multi-agent LLM systems: achieving agreement at the semantic level with certification. The findings highlight the importance of choosing the right admissibility instrument, which could influence future research on consensus and verification in LLM-based systems. The protocol emits three typed outcomes per round under a 2f+1 distinct-signer certificate. The paper proves a containment lemma showing no coverage separation from certificate-wrapped majority at matched deterministic guarantees, and reports a tie-break capture vulnerability with a four-line fix.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 14, 04:00

**Background**: In multi-agent systems built on large language models (LLMs), reaching agreement is often limited to label-level consensus, not meaning-level. Certifiable semantic agreement requires protocols that can verify that agents agree on the underlying semantics, not just surface labels. The admissibility instrument determines which inputs are considered valid for certification, and this paper shows it is the key factor in achieving coverage separation.

## [SDS-LoRA: Fixing Anisotropic Gradient Scaling in Low-Rank Adaptation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.16454) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces SDS-LoRA, a new low-rank parameterization that structurally decouples singular values from the backward pass, ensuring gradients flow only through orthonormal bases. This overcomes the anisotropic gradient scaling problem in standard LoRA, improving convergence and reducing the gap to full fine-tuning. This work addresses a fundamental geometric limitation of LoRA, a widely used parameter-efficient fine-tuning technique. By improving gradient fidelity, SDS-LoRA can enhance the performance of fine-tuned models across NLP and vision tasks, potentially making low-rank adaptation more competitive with full fine-tuning. SDS-LoRA ensures that the full fine-tuning gradient backpropagates only through the orthonormal bases of the low-rank matrices' subspaces, independent of their scales. Convergence analysis shows that while LoRA's convergence rate degrades with the condition number of the low-rank matrices, SDS-LoRA's remains independent of it.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 14, 04:00

**Background**: LoRA (Low-Rank Adaptation) is a parameter-efficient fine-tuning method that represents weight updates as low-rank matrices, reducing trainable parameters. However, this parameterization introduces anisotropic gradient scaling, where gradients are skewed toward dominant singular directions, distorting the full fine-tuning gradient. SDS-LoRA modifies the parameterization to decouple singular values from the backward pass, preserving gradient fidelity.

## [Linguistic Diversity Slows Consensus in Neural Cellular Automata](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.21202) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces 'languages' as sub-populations in a Neural Cellular Automaton (NCA) solving the density classification task, with tunable 'linguistic distance' between them. It finds that linguistic distance slows consensus and causes mild divergence, but training under diverse protocols improves robustness. This work challenges the common assumption of a single communication protocol in collective intelligence models, showing that heterogeneity alone can produce effects similar to human group studies. It offers insights for designing robust multi-agent systems and understanding consensus dynamics in diverse populations. The findings hold on both a ring and a two-dimensional grid, and are interpreted as Ising relaxation, where a foreign-language region acts as a boundary defect leaving the system in a higher-energy, partially ordered state. The paper is a preprint (arXiv:2606.21202) and not yet widely validated.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 14, 04:00

**Background**: Neural Cellular Automata (NCA) are models where neural networks learn local update rules for self-organization, as popularized by Distill's 'Growing Neural Cellular Automata'. The density classification task, also known as the majority problem, requires cells to determine the global majority from local interactions, a classic test of computational power in cellular automata. The Ising model, a mathematical model of ferromagnetism, describes spins on a lattice and is used here to interpret the system's behavior as relaxation to a higher-energy state.

## [OpenVLA Failure Detection Under Visual Shift: Strong Retrospective, Poor Calibration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.29699) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This study investigates whether OpenVLA's internal activations can serve as early warning signals for failures under visual distribution shift. It finds that a layer-16 logistic probe achieves high retrospective discrimination (AUROC 0.972) but poor operational calibration, averaging 3.32 false warnings per clean episode. This work addresses a critical safety issue in vision-language-action models (VLAs) like OpenVLA, which can fail unexpectedly under visual shifts. The findings highlight a key limitation in using internal activations for real-time failure detection, emphasizing the need for better calibration methods to ensure reliable deployment in robotics. The study uses the LIBERO-10 benchmark, freezing the OpenVLA policy and recording one MLP activation per step. Occlusion reduces task success from 57% to 17%, and the occlusion-trained probe transfers to camera-jitter episodes with AUROC 0.689, but the calibration check reveals excessive false alarms.

**Background**: OpenVLA is a 7B-parameter open-source vision-language-action model pretrained on 970k robot episodes from the Open X-Embodiment dataset, combining a Llama 2 language model with a visual encoder fusing DINOv2 and SigLIP features. LIBERO-10 is a benchmark for lifelong robot learning, consisting of 10 manipulation tasks. AUROC and AUPRC are standard metrics for evaluating binary classification performance, especially under class imbalance.

<a id="item-286"></a>
## [LLM Test Oracles Lack Formal Specifications, Systematic Review Finds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.05031) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This systematic literature review, following PRISMA 2020 guidelines, screened 2,436 records to 54 included studies (extended to 83 via snowballing) and classified LLM-based test oracles by their source of authority. It found that just over half of the corpus reaches a verdict with no specification at all. This matters because the source of authority determines how far a test oracle's verdict can be trusted, yet prior studies classified oracles by form or technique, not by this trust-governing property. The finding that most oracles lack formal specifications highlights a critical gap in automated software verification, urging developers to question what grounds an LLM oracle's verdict. The review reads the corpus along three axes: source of authority, form, and adjudication mechanism. It reveals that source and mechanism cross-cut rather than coincide, so labels like 'LLM-as-a-judge' describe how a verdict is produced, not why it should be trusted. Oracle quality is most often judged by resemblance to a known oracle rather than by fault detection capability.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 14, 04:00

**Background**: In software testing, a test oracle is a mechanism that determines whether a program behaves correctly for a given input. Large language models (LLMs) are increasingly used to generate or act as test oracles, but their verdicts can be based on learned patterns rather than explicit specifications. The PRISMA 2020 guidelines provide a standardized framework for reporting systematic reviews, ensuring transparency and completeness in the review process.

## [drXAI: XAI-Driven Data Reduction for Scalable Time Series Classification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.15774) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces drXAI, a novel methodology that repurposes explainable AI (XAI) attribution methods for data reduction in time series classification (TSC). It uses a GPU-accelerated classifier (Hydra) to generate local attributions, aggregates them into global feature importance scores, and applies an automated elbow-cut heuristic to select salient features without manual thresholds. This work addresses the scalability challenge in modern TSC, where state-of-the-art models like Transformers have quadratic complexity relative to sequence length and linear complexity relative to channels, making them computationally prohibitive for massive datasets. By enabling resource-intensive models like ConvTran to scale to previously inaccessible datasets, drXAI demonstrates that XAI can provide measurable performance gains beyond interpretability, offering a practical tool for handling large time series data. drXAI achieves between 80% and 90% data reduction on real-world datasets while maintaining classification accuracy comparable to models trained on the full dataset. On synthetic benchmarks, it successfully recovers ground-truth features where traditional baselines fail. The method is evaluated on both univariate and multivariate datasets, and all code and data are openly available.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 14, 04:00

**Background**: Time series classification (TSC) involves predicting a class label for sequences of data points over time. State-of-the-art TSC models, such as Transformers, often have high computational complexity, making them impractical for large datasets. Explainable AI (XAI) methods provide attributions that explain model predictions, but their use for data reduction is underexplored. The elbow method is a heuristic used to determine the optimal number of clusters or features by identifying the point of diminishing returns in a curve.

## [C4: Coordinating Adaptive Sampling and Early Exit in Diffusion LMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.28166) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces C4, a method that coordinates adaptive sampling and early exit in diffusion language models via two separate gates: Confidence-Verified Early Exit (CVEE) and Commit-Core-Then-Confirm (CCTC). On 12 zero-shot tasks with LLaDA and Dream, one frozen configuration removes 64–95% of decoding steps and delivers measured end-to-end speedups of 2.6 to 8.6 over full decoding. This work addresses a significant performance optimization problem in diffusion language models, a growing area in AI. By coordinating two acceleration axes, C4 could make diffusion-based models more practical for real-world applications, potentially influencing future efficient inference techniques. C4 uses two gates: CVEE decides when the sequence may stop based on confidence and sustained argmax stability, while CCTC decides which token positions a step may commit by borrowing an autoregressive freezing order. The method is evaluated on 12 zero-shot tasks with LLaDA and Dream, achieving significant speedups, and code is available on GitHub.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 14, 04:00

**Background**: Diffusion language models generate text by iteratively denoising corrupted sequences, offering parallel generation but often requiring many steps. Early exit is a technique that allows models to stop inference early when confident, and adaptive sampling adjusts the number of steps dynamically. Existing methods typically optimize only one axis, whereas C4 coordinates both.

A new arXiv preprint (2608.07070) uses a three-party evolutionary game model to show that coordinated incentives among regulators, AI enterprises, and users are necessary to effectively govern AI-generated misinformation. The study finds that unilateral regulation or market incentives alone are insufficient. This research addresses the growing challenge of AI-generated misinformation, which threatens information credibility and social trust. It provides a formal framework for designing coordinated policy mixes, potentially guiding regulators and enterprises in developing more effective governance strategies. The model incorporates heterogeneous rewards and punishments, and uses replicator equations to characterize evolutionary stability. It identifies critical thresholds for regulatory rewards, punishment intensity, enterprise reputation loss, and user adoption incentives that must be collectively surpassed to achieve a stable regime of real-information production.

## [Aftab Benchmark: CNN Encoders and Value Functions in PQN](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.07335) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces Aftab, a composite architecture that benchmarks eight CNN encoders and advanced value functions within Parallelized Q-Networks (PQN). On Atari-57, Aftab achieves an IQM Human-Normalized Score of 6.592, compared to 2.715 for standard PQN, and a 0.86 Probability of Improvement. This work addresses an underexplored area in deep reinforcement learning by systematically studying encoder architectures and value functions in a replay-free, parallelized setting. The significant performance gains demonstrate that careful architecture design can substantially improve efficiency and effectiveness, benefiting researchers and practitioners working with PQN-like algorithms. Aftab integrates the Hadamax encoding paradigm with categorical, ensemble, and dueling value heads, and is evaluated on Atari-57 and Procgen-Hard. On Procgen-Hard, Aftab achieves a normalized AUC of 0.541 versus 0.216 for PQN, while preserving the memory-efficient, replay-free training paradigm.

**Background**: Parallelized Q-Networks (PQN) is a recent deep reinforcement learning algorithm that enables off-policy value learning without experience replay buffers or target networks, leveraging environment vectorization and network normalization. The Hadamax encoder is a novel architecture that max-pools Hadamard products between GELU-activated parallel hidden layers, achieving state-of-the-art performance on Atari-57 when combined with PQN. This paper builds on these foundations to systematically explore architectural choices.

This paper introduces Private Etymology, a relational provenance model that records the lifecycle of dyad-specific symbolic expressions in long-term human-AI interaction, and proposes relational reuse to reactivate these expressions in later sessions without full re-explanation. In the prototype, a language model classifies conversational evidence, while deterministic local code decides whether a Shared Symbol can be updated, preventing free-form model confidence scores from directly altering persisted symbols.

## [Critic-Free Pretraining Boosts Offline-to-Online RL Fine-Tuning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.10473) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces Critic-Free Pretraining (CFP), a new paradigm for offline-to-online reinforcement learning that completely avoids training an offline critic. Instead, a freshly initialized critic is used during online fine-tuning, which prevents biased value estimates from hindering policy improvement. CFP addresses a known problem in offline-to-online RL—critic bias—and consistently matches or improves upon conventional methods across diverse tasks. This could lead to more efficient and reliable fine-tuning of RL agents, especially in challenging real-world applications where online interaction is costly. CFP is compatible with various mainstream offline-to-online algorithms and shows particularly pronounced gains on several challenging tasks. The paper is available on arXiv (2608.10473) and was announced as a replace-cross update.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 14, 04:00

**Background**: Offline-to-online reinforcement learning (O2O-RL) trains a policy on a static dataset and then fine-tunes it through online interaction. Traditional O2O pipelines reuse an offline-trained critic, but as the policy and data distribution shift, the critic's value estimates become misaligned, hurting fine-tuning. CFP avoids this by skipping offline critic training entirely, allowing a fresh critic to adapt without inherited bias.

## [REOPD: Reliability-Adaptive Reward Extrapolation for On-Policy Distillation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.11698) ⭐️ 7.0/10

REOPD introduces a reliability-adaptive reward extrapolation framework for on-policy distillation, which assigns a token-level coefficient λ_{b,t}=1+γ_b q_t to selectively extrapolate along reliable teacher-reference directions. It outperforms G-OPD on single-teacher mathematics and both domains in multi-teacher settings, while matching G-OPD on single-teacher code. This work addresses the critical issue of reward hacking in on-policy distillation, which can destabilize training and degrade performance. By providing a fine-grained, reliability-adaptive mechanism, REOPD offers a more robust and efficient alternative to existing global-coefficient methods, potentially improving LLM post-training and reinforcement learning pipelines. REOPD requires no verifier, reward model, value model, or extra rollout beyond standard OPD, making it lightweight and easy to integrate. The method combines a token-level compatibility weight with a batch-level adaptive budget, enabling dynamic adjustment of the extrapolation strength per token.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 14, 04:00

**Background**: On-policy distillation (OPD) trains a student model on its own trajectories under dense token-level supervision from a teacher, addressing the distribution mismatch in static corpus distillation. Reward extrapolation methods like ExOPD amplify the teacher-reference log-likelihood ratio to push the student beyond direct imitation, but they use a single global coefficient λ, which can cause reward hacking and require costly hyperparameter sweeps. REOPD builds on this by introducing a reliability-adaptive coefficient that selectively extrapolates only along reliable directions, mitigating these issues.

## [OGR-MARL Framework Boosts Heterogeneous USV Pursuit in Ports](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12995) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces OGR-MARL, an option-guided residual multi-agent reinforcement learning framework that is decoupled from specific MARL algorithms. It instantiates with MADDPG, MATD3, MAPPO, and MASAC, achieving a 75.0% capture rate with OGR-MASAC in an abstract Xiazhimen port-waterway scenario. This framework addresses the challenge of cooperative pursuit by heterogeneous USVs in constrained port waterways, a niche but safety-critical domain. By integrating rule-guided behaviors with residual learning, it improves sample efficiency and generalization, potentially advancing autonomous maritime operations. OGR-MARL integrates shared evader belief, role-conditioned option targets, adaptive rule penalties, and residual policy learning. Zero-shot transfer to a QGIS/AIS-informed Xiazhimen map showed promising results without retraining, indicating strong generalization potential.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 14, 04:00

**Background**: Multi-agent reinforcement learning (MARL) extends reinforcement learning to multiple agents interacting in a shared environment. Heterogeneous USVs (unmanned surface vehicles) have different capabilities, and cooperative pursuit involves intercepting evaders under navigation and traffic constraints. OGR-MARL combines rule-based behaviors with residual learning to avoid exploring from scratch, improving efficiency.

## [Egocentric Vision Aids Freezing of Gait Detection, IMU TCN Still Best](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13283) ⭐️ 6.0/10

A new study from arXiv (2608.13283) evaluates egocentric vision and wearable IMUs for context-aware freezing of gait (FOG) detection in Parkinson's patients at home. The IMU-based temporal convolutional network (TCN) achieved the best event-detection performance (42.3 F1, 83.0 AUROC), outperforming V-JEPA2 ego-video features (32.6 F1, 77.2 AUROC). This research highlights the potential of egocentric vision to add contextual information to wearable-based clinical motion understanding, which could improve FOG detection in daily living. It also shows that simple IMU-based models can outperform large foundation model features, informing future system design for home monitoring. The study used synchronized egocentric video, wearable IMUs, and expert-annotated FOG labels from 13 Parkinson's participants in their homes, with leave-one-subject-out evaluation. Although ego-video alone did not outperform IMU sensing, it showed above-chance discrimination, and qualitative analyses suggest egocentric vision captures FOG-relevant information independent of IMUs.

**Background**: Freezing of gait (FOG) is a common and debilitating symptom of Parkinson's disease, characterized by sudden inability to move forward. Detecting FOG in daily living is challenging because similar inertial patterns can occur during intentional stops or object interactions. Egocentric vision, captured by head-mounted cameras, provides first-person context that may help disambiguate these situations. Temporal convolutional networks (TCNs) are a type of deep learning model well-suited for time-series data like IMU signals.

## [SLMs and Edge Computing Enhance Virtual Agent Cognition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.13420) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This paper presents an exploratory evaluation of using Small Language Models (SLMs) on edge devices to support the 'Think' and 'Memory' processes of embodied virtual agents within the Cognitive Embodied Agent Architecture (CEAA). This work is significant because it addresses the challenge of deploying cognitively capable virtual agents in resource-constrained edge environments, potentially enabling more persistent and adaptive agents in virtual worlds and Metaverse applications. It highlights the growing trend of using SLMs for on-device AI, which could reduce reliance on cloud infrastructure and improve privacy and latency. The system was evaluated on an NVIDIA Jetson Orin NX, a powerful edge computing platform, using Qwen2.5 models of different sizes. Simulation experiments measured routing accuracy, memory-read performance, and latency, showing that an SLM-driven prototype can partially implement selected CEAA processes for interactive immersive experiences.

Small Language Models (SLMs) are compact, purpose-built transformers that can run efficiently on edge devices, making them suitable for resource-constrained environments. Edge computing brings AI inference closer to the data source, reducing latency and bandwidth usage while improving privacy.

## [FMG-Bench: Benchmarking LLMs for Theological Triage and Pastoral Guidance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.12324) ⭐️ 6.0/10

The paper introduces FMG-Bench, a benchmark with 120 scenarios for evaluating LLMs in Christian theological triage and pastoral guidance. It evaluates 14 models across 8,792 responses, showing that a structured harness improves performance by +3.96 points on average, with a +10.8 point gain in escalation appropriateness. This benchmark addresses a growing real-world use of LLMs for faith-related counsel, providing a structured way to measure safety and doctrinal accuracy. It highlights the importance of guided settings in improving model behavior, especially for safety-critical pastoral situations, and offers a tool for developers and religious communities. The benchmark includes three guided instruction settings and measures stability (92.88 to 98.02) when questions are reworded. It finds that comparing perspectives helps with secondary doctrines but can be counterproductive for primary doctrines or urgent pastoral situations. The benchmark is explicitly a measurement tool, not an endorsement of AI as pastoral authority.

The framework was evaluated with random forest, SVM, and multilayer perceptron classifiers under EEG-only and EEG+EMG settings. It highlights the effectiveness of ensemble and reranking strategies for fine-grained cross-modal retrieval tasks, which could influence future solutions in surveillance and public safety applications. The framework builds upon a strong retrieval backbone and progressively integrates heterogeneous vision-language models. A disagreement-aware routing mechanism identifies ambiguous queries based on ensemble consensus and selectively invokes a large vision-language model (VLM) for cross-encoder reranking.

The paper introduces EGRL, a novel graph neural network framework for RNA-protein interaction prediction that incorporates implicit meta-path learning, a multi-relation-aware attention mechanism, a graph generator for soft edge prediction, and a multi-feature fusion predictor. It achieves state-of-the-art performance in cold-start settings, with an AUROC of 0.867 and AUPR of 0.861 on unknown molecules, improving by 8.6% and 5.0% respectively over prior methods. This work addresses a critical limitation in existing GNN-based RPI prediction methods, which often struggle with data sparsity and generalization to unknown molecules. By improving cold-start performance, EGRL could accelerate drug discovery and functional genomics research by enabling more reliable predictions for novel RNA-protein interactions. EGRL is jointly trained with a primary task loss and an auxiliary generator loss, and it uses implicit meta-path learning to avoid handcrafted paths. The framework was evaluated on four benchmark datasets, showing competitive overall performance and superior generalization in cold-start settings.

**Background**: RNA-protein interactions (RPIs) are crucial for cellular functions, but traditional wet-lab detection is costly and time-consuming. Deep learning, especially graph neural networks (GNNs), offers an efficient alternative by modeling RPI networks as graphs. However, existing GNN methods often rely on homogeneous graphs or predefined meta-paths, limiting their ability to handle data sparsity and cold-start scenarios where unknown molecules appear. EGRL addresses these issues by learning relational semantics implicitly and generating soft edges to support cold-start nodes.

## [DiffImaginE: Diffusion-Based Entity Type Verification for MNER](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.03025) ⭐️ 6.0/10

DiffImaginE introduces a novel approach to multimodal named entity recognition (MNER) by using conditional latent diffusion to verify entity types, replacing the deterministic verifier with a diffusion-based scorer that ranks type hypotheses via denoising error as a surrogate for type-conditional likelihood. This work bridges diffusion models and entity typing, offering a probabilistic framework that could improve MNER accuracy and inspire future research in multimodal understanding. It demonstrates consistent gains over deterministic baselines, potentially advancing social media content analysis and knowledge graph construction. DiffImaginE uses classifier-free guidance and Min-SNR weighting, directly supervises per-type diffusion scores as classification logits, and employs antithetic sampling to reduce Monte Carlo variance. Experiments on Twitter-2015 and Twitter-2017 show consistent gains over a matched deterministic ImaginE control, supported by ablations and paired significance tests.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 14, 04:00

**Background**: Multimodal named entity recognition (MNER) determines whether candidate spans and entity types are supported by both textual and visual evidence. Traditional imagine-and-compare verifiers map each (span, type) pair to a single predicted visual feature, which compresses diverse visual realisations and lacks explicit probabilistic semantics. Diffusion models, such as conditional latent diffusion, generate data by iteratively denoising a latent representation, and the denoising error can serve as a proxy for likelihood. This paper leverages that property to rank entity type hypotheses.

## [Bayesian Optimization Automates Termination of Inelastic Neutron Scattering Experiments](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.16946) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This paper proposes a Bayesian-optimization-based method to automatically determine when to terminate inelastic neutron-scattering experiments by optimizing bin widths. The method terminates the experiment when the optimal bin widths become smaller than the target resolutions, and numerical experiments show it reduces search cost to about 10% of an exhaustive search. This work addresses the inefficient use of valuable beam time in neutron scattering experiments, a critical resource at large-scale facilities. By automating termination decisions, it can improve experimental efficiency and reduce data oversampling, benefiting researchers in condensed matter physics and materials science. The method uses Bayesian optimization to efficiently compute optimal bin widths for multidimensional histograms, and terminates when these widths fall below target resolutions. In tests with real data, even downsampled to 1/5, the optimal bin widths were comparable to resolution limits, indicating excessive measurement; Bayesian optimization reduced search cost to approximately 10% of exhaustive search.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Aug 14, 04:00

**Background**: Inelastic neutron scattering is a technique used to study the dynamic properties of materials, such as magnetic excitations and lattice vibrations. Experiments collect event data in four dimensions, and binning this data into histograms is essential for analysis. Optimizing bin widths is crucial for balancing resolution and statistical noise, and Bayesian optimization is a powerful method for efficiently optimizing expensive black-box functions, making it suitable for this task.
